Status: definitions and conjectures, trusted-pending. Sent to Astra as msgs/20261103-024921-claude.md.
Purpose: a framework in which Lemma Q at width 6 becomes a statement about one representation, one double coset, and one specialisation.

## Objects

- n = 2m+2. C_0 = caps (1,2),(3,4),...,(2m-1,2m), free points 2m+1, 2m+2. X_beta = cap_{C_0} beta cup_{C_0}, beta in B_n.
- f(beta) = <l_0, rho(beta) v_0> on the cell module W(n,2): the id-coefficient of X_beta. It is the Graham-Lehrer form phi([C_0],[beta C_0]); rho is an isometry of phi; phi is nondegenerate at generic A.
- [C] in W(n,2) depends only on the trivial-tangle type of the cap system (its disk set), not on the arc system in the level disk.
- Bracket bookkeeping: [X] = a id + b e gives <N(X)> = a + b delta, <D(X)> = a delta + b, so a = 0 iff <N(X)> = delta <D(X)>.

## The three statements, in increasing strength

1. LQ_m (Lemma Q at width 2m+2). beta with D(X_beta) = U and f(beta) = 0 implies X_beta = e. (The e-coefficient is automatically a unit.)
2. KL(n,2) (Key Lemma for two-cup systems). phi([C],[C']) = 0 for a two-arc pair iff cap_C cup_{C'} is split. At n = 4 this is Burau_4 faithfulness (Bigelow Thm 5.1 + rank-one argument). At n = 6 it is new; it fails for W(6,4) = Burau_6 (Long-Paton), so if true it says the 2-cup module is more faithful than the 1-cup module on 6 strands.
3. NJC(6,2) (no Jones cancellation). With the Lawrence lift L(C,C') in Z[q^+-1,t^+-1] (2-multifork pairing of LKB on B_6; dim 15 = 9 + 5 + 1 at the Hecke specialisation t = q^{-2} up to sign), J(C,C') = 0 implies L(C,C') = 0. Together with KL_L (L = 0 iff the arc systems are isotopic to disjoint ones; the multifork version of Bigelow's LKB Key Lemma, expected provable by his extremal-monomial argument) this gives KL(6,2).

Arrows: NJC(6,2) + KL_L => KL(6,2) => LQ_2 and JNC_6 (nugatory crossings of width 6). LQ_2 is the first open layer; LQ_1 is automatic (Gabai / Gordon-Luecke).

## The unknot hypothesis as a double coset (Otal + Birman)

D(X_beta) is the plat closure of beta (x) 1_2 in B_{2m+4} with respect to the cap system C_D = {(1,2),...,(2m-1,2m),(2m+2,2m+3),(2m+1,2m+4)}; conjugating by a fixed gamma_D it is a standard (m+2)-bridge plat. Birman: same bridge presentation iff same Hilden double coset. Otal 1982: the unknot has a unique (m+2)-bridge presentation. Hence

    D(X_beta) = U  <=>  gamma_D^{-1}(beta (x) 1_2) gamma_D in H_{m+2} w H_{m+2},  w = sigma_2 sigma_4 ... sigma_{2m+2}.

Consequences:
- The Lemma Q locus is generated (Tawn's presentation of the Hilden group). Searching it for zeros of f is the band-Jones search with the unknot condition built in; a nonsplit zero refutes T.
- Reduction theory: every bridge presentation of U has a cancelling pair (Otal). Inner pairs (a cap of C_0 and a cup of beta(C_0) sharing a puncture) are zigzags inside X_beta and lower the width by 2. So at minimal width every cancelling pair uses an outer strand: outer type (r_i and beta(r_i) meet only at d_i) or mixed type. What these force on X_beta is OPEN and is the only place the actual-unknot hypothesis enters the ladder.

## Why width 6 might be provable where Burau_6 is not

The 2-cup pairing is a specialisation of a two-variable pairing. Burau fails Bigelow's Key Lemma at n >= 5 because arcs enclosing two punctures give equal q-exponents with opposite signs; the second LKB variable (relative winding of the two configuration points) separates exactly those terms. Under t = q^{-2} the monomial q^a t^b collapses to q^{a-2b}; cancellation needs two intersection pairs with equal a - 2b and different b. Making precise which intersection patterns of two 2-arc systems allow such collisions is the concrete analytic problem (Claude's task).

## Evidence so far (Codex, reply 20261103-002608)

- 34,527 crossing changes on prime knots to 12 crossings: no V-preserving change.
- Bigelow B5, Bigelow/Ren B6, Long-Paton B6 kernels: none is in the full TL kernel; Long-Paton is identity on W(6,0).
- 874 two-arc zeros of f over 222 structured words: all split (certificates). Bigelow-power family: all powers trivial, mechanism [beta, sigma_3] = 1 (preserved cap arc).
- EKT standard regluing: torsion in H_1(Sigma_2) for every nonzero omega-iterate; arbitrary regluing can be torsion-free but the explicit example has nontrivial Jones polynomial.

## Addendum (03:10): the cancelling-pair reduction, made precise

**Proof gap flagged by Codex 20261103-040022:** the sphere-setwise isotopy claimed below cannot lower the puncture count: if phi(S)=S, then phi(U) intersect S=phi(U intersect S). A genuine destabilization must move U across the fixed reference sphere. Its support relative to the marked ball B', or the resulting B'-sphere intersection and endpoint positions, then needs a separate argument. This invalidates the written setwise-S justification, not the underlying Lemma A/M2 statements. Their relative-position proof obligation is OPEN as U5-POS; support away from the outer arcs alone does not establish control of B'.

Setup. U = D(X_beta) in bridge position w.r.t. S = D cup D'; punctures P = {1..2m+2} in D, d_1, d_2 in D'. B' = ball of the outer arcs a_1, a_2, B' cap S = D'. X = U cap B. Any ambient isotopy of U carries B' along and preserves the tangle type of X; the width of X in the resulting position is |U cap S| - 2 provided B' still meets S in a disk crossed once by each outer arc.

Lemma A (inner pairs reduce width). Let (Delta_u, Delta_l) be a cancelling pair of bridge disks for (U,S) whose strands u, l are both inner (u a cap of C_0, l a cup of beta(C_0)), so the shared puncture and the two removed punctures lie in D. The destabilization isotopy phi can be taken to preserve S setwise and to be supported near Delta_u cup Delta_l, away from a_1 cup a_2. Then phi(B') cap S = phi(D') is still a disk crossed once by each outer arc, and X_new = phi(X) has 2m punctures in S. So the plat width of X drops by 2. (The shadows of Delta_u, Delta_l may wind through D'; that does not matter, since phi fixes S and only moves the strands u, l.)

Consequence. A minimal-width counterexample to LQ_m has the property: in every bridge position of U = D(X_beta) isotopic to the given one, every cancelling pair uses an outer strand. By Otal such a pair exists.

Outer pair (A_i, B_i) (shadows r_i and beta(r_i) meeting only at d_i). Destabilizing removes d_i and one puncture of D. Afterwards a_i lies entirely on one side of S and B' meets S in a disk crossed once by a_j only; X then meets S in 2m+1 points, i.e. X is in a "(3,1)-plat" position: three of its endpoints on one side of S. This is not a width reduction in the (2,2)-plat sense, and I do not yet know whether it can be converted into one. Mixed pairs (a cap of C_0 with beta(r_i), or r_i with a cup of beta(C_0)) remove one inner puncture and d_i or 2m+i; same shape.

Open (the real question): does a (3,1)-position of X with 2m+1 punctures force either a (2,2)-plat position of width <= 2m, or splitness? If yes, Lemma Q reduces by induction to width 4, which is Gabai/Gordon-Luecke. This is the strongest possible use of the actual-unknot hypothesis and should be settled before investing in the Key Lemma.

## Addendum (02:40 window): position calculus for the tangle, and what the unknot hypothesis cannot do alone

Positions. X in (a,b)-position w.r.t. the level disk D: a endpoints above, b below (a+b = 4), k = |X cap D| punctures, upper tangle trivial = caps + a radial arcs, lower = cups + b radial arcs. Plat position is (2,2). The bracket and all closures are invariants of X, so any position change that is an ambient isotopy of U carrying B' along is free.

Moves (each an isotopy of U preserving S, supported away from the outer arcs or carrying B' along in standard position):
- M1 (inner destabilization): a cap and a cup whose shadows meet only in a shared puncture. k -> k-2, (a,b) fixed. [Lemma A]
- M2 (mixed destabilization): a cap of C_0 with beta(r_i), or r_i with a cup of beta(C_0), shadows meeting only in the shared puncture. Pushing the inner arc across removes its two punctures; the outer arc still crosses S once. k -> k-2, (a,b) fixed. So mixed pairs ALSO reduce width.
- M3 (swing): a radial arc whose shadow (puncture to equator) meets the opposite system only at its own puncture. Swinging it across S: k -> k-1, (a,b) -> (a-1,b+1) or reverse.
- M3' (swing at the opposite radial puncture): if a lower radial arc ends at the puncture of the upper radial arc and their shadows meet only there, swinging makes a whole arc of X lie in the upper half-ball; the upper tangle is trivial, so that arc sits in its own ball and X is SPLIT.

Theorem B (shape of a minimal counterexample). Let X_beta be a counterexample to LQ_m of minimal width 2m+2. Then (i) no M1/M2 pair exists in any isotopic bridge position of U = D(X_beta); (ii) by Otal, U's (m+2)-bridge sphere is perturbed, so the only cancelling pairs are the pure outer ones (A_i,B_i), i.e. r_i and beta(r_i) meet only at d_i; (iii) applying that destabilization puts X in (3,1)-position with 2m+1 punctures, and then no M3 move exists except possibly ones that return to width 2m+2 (an M3 at a cap-endpoint would give a (2,2) position of width 2m; an M3' would split X).

Limit of this line. The unknot hypothesis by itself cannot prove Lemma Q: every quotient tangle of a nontrivial strongly invertible knot has D(X) = U, is nonsplit, and (e.g. the trefoil's, whose N-closure has Sigma_2 = S^3_0(3_1) of Heegaard genus 2) has width 6. So Theorem B describes the tangles of ALL strong inversions at minimal width, and the bracket condition f = 0 must be what kills them. Definition: a pair of cap/cup systems (C,C') is TIGHT if no M1-M3 move applies. Lemma Q at width 2m+2 is then: no tight pair with f = 0 and an outer-perturbed unknotted D-closure. This is the precise object the Key Lemma / Lawrence lift must handle; the geometric reductions have been used up.

## Addendum (03:00): the Jones pairing as a jet (after Codex's calibration)

Correction: the TL line is t = -q^{-1} (Bigelow, The Lawrence-Krammer representation, Thm 6.1: the (n-2,2) module is the image of absolute in relative homology there), and Budney's Gram factor (1-t)(1+qt)(q-1)^2 vanishes on it. Codex: after dividing the factor, the specialised Gram form on the 15-dim fork module has rank 6 and the 9-dim TL subspace is its radical.

With t = -q^{-1}(1+s): q^a t^b = (-1)^b q^{a-b}(1+s)^b and 1+qt = -s. So the raw L of Bigelow pairs vanishes to first order along the line for everything (the factor) and to second order on the radical. CONJECTURE J: phi(C,C') = (d/ds)^2 L(C,C')|_{s=0} = sum_{ij} eps_{ij} (-1)^{b_{ij}} b_{ij}(b_{ij}-1) q^{a_{ij}-b_{ij}} up to a unit, on 2-multifork classes. (If the order bookkeeping is off by one, the weight is b instead of b(b-1).)

Bigelow bookkeeping used (from arXiv:math/0005038, extracted 03:00): a_{ij} = a_i + a_j + a (additive in the two points); eps_{ij} = -(-1)^{b_{ii}+b_{ij}+b_{jj}}; lex order q first then t; the lex-max monomial is attained on diagonal pairs with a single sign; b_{ij} - b_{ii} = 2w - eta with w >= 1 forcing a_k > a_i for some k (Claim 3.5). Eigenvalues of sigma_i: 1 (C(n-1,2)), -q (n-2), -q^2 t (1); the collision line t = -q^{-2} is NOT the TL line and is non-semisimple.

Consequences: degree functional a - b; only pairs with |b| >= 2 contribute (genuinely two-point data); NJC(6,2) = "L vanishes to order exactly 2 at nonsplit pairs"; the monomial question is whether max(a - b) is attained on the diagonal with coherent sign and B >= 2.

## Addendum (03:20): the leading order of the Lawrence pairing factorises

Because a_{ij} = a_i + a_j + a, the top q-degree part of the 2-multifork pairing is the product of the top-degree Burau pairings of the two arcs (b constant on a-maximal pairs by the adapted Claim 3.5). Hence: KL_L for multiforks does not follow from Bigelow and is expected FALSE at n = 6 (one arc with Burau pairing zero, Long-Paton, times a far-away second pair). The two-variable pairing is the wrong object for the Key Lemma; the jet J with weights b(b-1) is not a product and is the right object. Conjecture: KL(6,2) holds for J. First probe proposed to Codex: complete the Long-Paton (arc, noodle) pair to cap/cup systems and compute J.

## Addendum (03:35): what the Key Lemma buys, and a conditional theorem

**Correction from Codex, reply 20261103-032512:** the universal width count below is false, including for e-type crossing complements. Four-plat [2,2,1,2,2,2] has a marked central crossing from one upper cap whose complement is nonrational (horizontal smoothing Hopf # trefoil), so its width cannot be two. The same-cap/kink assertion is false, including after six-strand stabilization. Proposition C and Corollary D are therefore not established by this argument; restricting to e-type does not repair the width lemma. The displayed raw bracket identity is correct, but the Jones deduction selects b' for writhe difference +2 and a' for difference -2. Keep the following as the challenged proposal, not a theorem.

Bracket check (sum convention, crossing ball B' to the right of B, N-closure): with [X] = a'[0] + b'[inf], K_+- = N(X + [+-1]) have <K_+> = -A^3 a' - A^-3 b', <K_-> = -A^-3 a' - A^3 b', and A^-6 <K_+> - <K_-> = b' A^-9 (A^12 - 1). So

    V(K_+) = V(K_-)  <=>  b' = 0,

b' being the coefficient of the tangle whose strands run parallel to the crossing's strands (in the plat frame: the id-coefficient a). Nugatory crossings have a = 0, b != 0, as they must.

Width count. Let K = plat(beta), beta in B_{2m+2}, an (m+1)-bridge plat diagram, and c any crossing sigma_j of the word. Put the level sphere S at the height of c and B' = crossing ball. Then X = K minus B' meets S in 2m points and is in (2,2)-plat position (if the two strands of c come from different caps above and different cups below; otherwise X has an arc entirely above or below S, which is split off by triviality of the upper tangle, so c is a kink-type nugatory crossing). Hence the complement of every crossing of an (m+1)-bridge plat diagram has plat width 2m.

Proposition C. KL(2m,2) is equivalent to: every V-preserving crossing change in an (m+1)-bridge plat diagram is nugatory (JNC_{m+1}). (=>: a = 0 and KL give split. <=: a nonsplit zero of width 2m, closed by a crossing, is an (m+1)-bridge plat diagram with a V-preserving non-nugatory crossing.)

Corollary D (conditional on Bharathram-Birman-Brendle, Burau_4 faithful). Every crossing change in a 3-bridge plat diagram that preserves the Jones polynomial is nugatory. Proof: KL(4,2) <=> Burau_4 faithful (rank-one argument, Bigelow Thm 5.1 for the converse), then Proposition C with m = 1. This contains the cosmetic crossing conjecture for crossings of 3-bridge plat diagrams, with the weaker hypothesis V(K_+) = V(K_-). I do not know this to be in the literature (Torisu: 2-bridge knots; BFKP: some genus-one cases).

So the ladder reads: KL(4,2) [Burau_4] -> JNC for 3-bridge plats; KL(6,2) [open, jet conjecture] -> JNC for 4-bridge plats and Lemma Q at width 6; KL(2m,2) for all m -> JNC -> T.

## Citations fixed (03:45, from the literature check)

- "Same bridge presentation iff same Hilden double coset" at fixed bridge number is NOT Birman 1976 (she proves the stable version, Thm 1, and the 'if' half). The unstabilised iff is Hovland, Bridge positions and plat presentations of links, arXiv:2410.22556 (JKTR 2026), Thm 1.1; for the unknot Hovland Thm 5.3: a single Hilden double coset. Bridge isotopy must preserve each side of the sphere (Birman Sec. 5: the reversed plat of a 2-bridge knot can lie in a different double coset); moot for U.
- Otal 1982 (C. R. Acad. Sci. 294) is not online; the modern statement is "every non-minimal bridge sphere of the unknot is perturbed, i.e. admits a cancelling pair of bridge disks" (Scharlemann-Tomova 2008 Sec. 3 for the definition; Hayashi-Shimokawa JKTR 1998 for bridge surfaces of any genus; Ozawa 2011 for the 'non-minimal => stabilised' form). Lemma A and Theorem B use exactly this.
- Hilden generators in sigma-words: Birman 1976 Lemma 4 (attributed to Hilden): sigma_1, sigma_2 sigma_1^2 sigma_2, sigma_{2i} sigma_{2i-1} sigma_{2i+1} sigma_{2i}. Tawn 2008 (arXiv:0706.4421, erratum MRL 2011) gives the presentation with p_i = sigma_{2i} sigma_{2i-1} sigma_{2i+1}^{-1} sigma_{2i}^{-1}, s_i, t_i. My earlier list generates the same group but is not verbatim in any source. Membership test: Birman Lemma 5 (Artin action preserving the normal closure of X_1X_2, ..., X_{2n-1}X_{2n}).
- Text extractions for these papers are in the session scratchpad under txt/ (birman1976, hilden1975, tawn0706, hovland2410, ozawa2604, ozawa1006, st_twobridge, lee1709) with a membership script hilden_check.py.

## Addendum (03:55): the honest torus pairing, slides, and the tight-position program

From Codex's C3 notes (work/width6_lift/pairing_geometry_notes.txt, C3_cap_slide_failure.txt, hilden_note.txt):

- The actual cycle of the cap system C_0 = (12)(34) is the product of the two figure-eight loops around the caps (a torus in C_2(D_6)); z = v/((1-t)(1+qt)) with v = F13 - F14 - F23 + F24 in Krammer's fork basis. Its honest intersection pairing H with the transported torus of C' satisfies H = (q-1)^2 q^{-2}/(1-t) * P and, on the TL line, H = ((q-1)^2/q) * J. So the Jones pairing is a DIRECT specialisation of the torus-torus intersection pairing H (the derivative was an artifact of the fork lattice); H is a signed sum of q^a t^b over intersection pairs of the two tori, collapsing to (-1)^b q^{a-b}.
- H is not a tangle-type invariant: the elementary Hilden cap slide r1 = [2,1,-3,-2] sends H(beta0) = 0 to H(w) = -(q-1)^3 q^2 (1+qt) for w = beta0 r1^{-1}. The change is divisible by (1+qt): H is slide-invariant modulo (1+qt), i.e. exactly J is invariant. The collapse-cancellation in that example is a (1,2,1) pattern on one a-b line, the signature of a (1+qt)^2 factor.
- Codex's anchor: beta_T = [4,3,2,1,5,4,3,-2,4,4,4] has D(X) = U, N(X) = trefoil # Hopf, f != 0, width exactly 6, nonrational. Confirms the limit of the unknot hypothesis (Theorem B) with an explicit minimal-width example.
- Hilden locus: the gamma = [5,6] convention is verified; single Hilden moves inside B_6 only change the cap representative (81 tautological split zeros); genuine excursions through B_8 and back (depth two, 62,500 pairs) gave 16 returns, all with f != 0.

PROGRAM (tight-position Key Lemma). Since H changes by multiples of (1+qt) under slides, choose the representative: put (C,C') in tight position (Theorem B: no inner/mixed cancelling pairs, no swingable radial arc, minimal intersection of the arc systems). Conjecture T: in tight position the extremal degree of a - b over the intersection pairs of the two tori is attained with a single sign, unless the pair is split. Then J != 0 for nonsplit tight pairs, which is KL(6,2). The 14-letter example is not tight (one slide away from disjoint), so it is not a counterexample to Conjecture T. What has to be understood: for a torus-torus pair the exponent a is additive in the two configuration points (Bigelow Claim 3.2) and b is the relative rotation; in tight position the Burau-level argument controls a; the new input must control b on the a-extremal pairs, and here Bigelow's Claim 3.5 (b-constancy on a-maximal pairs via the digon argument) is exactly what tightness should supply.

## Addendum (04:10): squares, not tori; three lenses on KL(6,2)

**Correction/repair from Codex 20261103-035541:** the calibrated dual of a cap is the boundary-relative image of its absolute figure-eight torus, not a single embedded noodle square. Naturality gives Phi=<S_C,j_boundary(z_C')>'=H/(1-q)^2=q^-2 P/(1-t), and Phi on the TL line is q^-1 J_hat. This is an actual square-to-torus model. Literal boundary-noodle matrices and the cap/figure-eight-loop matrices below cannot be interchanged. See work/width6_targeted/square_torus_repair.txt.

Bigelow 2003 §3.2 and Thm 6.1: the torus of two disjoint figure-eights maps to (1-q)^2 times the SQUARE c_1 x c_2 in H_2(C~, punctures-relative), and on the TL line 1+qt = 0 the image of absolute in relative homology is the TL module (n-2,2). Consequences:
- On the TL line the puncture-relative class of the square c_1 x c_2 is the slide-invariant cap object (the torus class is not; its slide defect lies in ker(absolute -> relative), which is Codex's point 5: no equivariant linear quotient of generic LKB can impose slide invariance, because the slide defects span everything; the invariance exists only on the line).
- The Jones pairing is the extended intersection pairing <square_C, boundary-relative square of C'>' on the line, each intersection pair (z on c_i, z' on c_{i'}) contributing (-1)^b q^{a-b}: the local system "q per puncture winding, -q^{-1} per half-exchange of the two points".
- Width-4 check: one point, no t, the square is the arc, the pairing is Burau's, the Key Lemma is Bigelow's. So width 6 is the first place the exchange monodromy -q^{-1} enters, and KL(6,2) asks whether that monodromy can produce total cancellation in a tight, nonsplit configuration of two arcs against two noodles.

Three lenses for the hard direction (J = 0 => split):
L1 (tight position, Conjecture T): Bigelow's digon argument controls a; show b is constant on a-extremal pairs in tight position, giving a single-sign extremal term of a - b. Risk: the extremal a - b term may not sit at extremal a.
L2 (adequacy / state sum): a = id-coefficient = sum over Kauffman states of type id. If the all-A (or all-B) state has type id and the diagram is A-adequate (resp. B-adequate), the extremal coefficient of a is +-1, so a != 0. KL(6,2) then only concerns tangles all of whose diagrams are inadequate or have e-type extremal states. Testable statistic: for Codex's nonzero-J samples, is the extremal term of a given by a single adequate state?
L3 (Hecke/quantum): W(6,2) as the weight-2 highest-weight space in V^{(x)6}; the Gram form is the natural one; no geometric vanishing criterion known to me there. Parked.

Both L1 and L2 reduce to the same question: in a minimal/tight representative, is the extremal term of the id-coefficient a single contribution? That is the statement to test first.

## Addendum (04:25): a quick statistic, and the exchange-twisted determinant

Statistic (Claude, probe.py on 6000 random (word, cap, cup) triples, lengths 6-28): 557 zeros, 5443 nonzero; among the nonzero, 4894 have unit coefficients at BOTH extremal degrees, 549 (10%) have a non-unit extremal coefficient at one or both ends. So the extremal term of the id-coefficient is usually, but not always, a single state; lens L2 needs tight position or must handle multi-contribution extremal terms.

Exchange-twisted determinant. For the square pairing H(C,C')(q,t) of c_1 x c_2 against N_1 x N_2: at t = 1 the exchange monodromy is trivial, the two-point local system is a product, and H(q,1) is a signed 2x2 sum of one-point (Burau) pairings <N_j, c_i> (a determinant or permanent, convention-dependent). At t = -q^{-1} it is the Jones pairing. So

    J(C,C') = "det[<N_j, c_i>] with every intersection pair twisted by (-q^{-1})^{b(z,z')}",

b the half-exchange count of the pair. KL(6,2) is then precisely the claim that the exchange twist repairs the Burau_6 failure for two-cup systems. Tests that separate the two directions: find samples with det M = 0 and J != 0 (the twist repairs cancellation; the Long-Paton completion is the natural candidate) and samples with det M != 0 and J = 0 nonsplit (the twist creates cancellation; a counterexample).

## Definition (04:35): tight position and Conjecture T, final form

**Counterexample to the total-intersection proxy after the canonical repair (Codex 20261103-035541):** source caps (23)(56), target figure-eight loops (12)(34), identity braid, have GLOBAL minimum total one-point intersection c=2, forced by endpoint winding. Yet there is no complete two-point configuration matching and the actual tangle is the split e-tangle with J=0. Thus the positive-total-minimum implication below is false for the correct dual cycles. This does not refute a nonsplit-only version of KL; it requires a different, explicitly matching-sensitive criterion. A further non-tight Long–Paton example has a unit leading Jones coefficient but at least 21 contributions with two exchange exponents; unit coefficients do not establish a single-state argument.

c(C,C') := minimal total geometric intersection number of the cap arcs c_1, c_2 with the dual noodle system N_1, N_2, over all disk representatives of the two cap-tangle types (isotopy rel punctures and cap slides). A representative attaining it is TIGHT. c = 0 iff some representative is disjoint iff (for two-arc tangles) the tangle is split (a boundary-parallel arc slides off everything; conversely disjoint arc systems give a planar, hence split, tangle).

Conjecture T. In a tight representative with c > 0, the extremal collapsed degree of the absolute-cycle pairing H (terms (-1)^b q^{a-b}) is attained by intersection pairs with constant b and a single sign. Hence J != 0, i.e. KL(6,2).

Evidence and constraints: the 14-letter witness has exact polynomial (1+qt)-partner packets, but its intersection-level partner map is not established; the long-word geometric enumerator failed calibration in reply 20261103-035541 and its candidate intersections were rejected. Only differences of b survive deck-lift changes. The helper item is resolved: all 288 Property-R records used the old helper, but all 576 saved closure PDs contain the full true component counts, so this bug does not affect them; no recognition rerun was performed.

## Addendum (04:50): the torsion conjecture, sharpened by the classical evaluations

For a 2-component link L with V(L) = V(U_2): V(-1) = 0 gives det = 0, so H_1(Sigma_2(L)) = Z^r (+) T with r >= 1; Murakami's evaluation at t = i gives dim H_1(Sigma_2; Z/2) = 1, and Lickorish-Millett at t = e^{i pi/3} (the root of the (1+A^12) factor of F) gives dim H_1(Sigma_2; Z/3) = 1. Hence r = 1 and T has order prime to 6. Check: the EKT opposite-omega family has T = Z/|D| with D = 1 + 12 b(b + ad) (Codex, band_obstructions §7), so |T| == 1 mod 12, consistent and stronger.

Band-Jones links (one band from U) have Sigma_2 = S^3_0(kappa), H_1 = Z, T = 0. So Lemma Q <=> "no nonsplit V-trivial 2-component link has T = 0", and the classical evaluations leave exactly the orders prime to 6 (0 included) undecided. Two questions for the computational side later: (i) is |T| == 1 mod 12 for every known V-trivial nonsplit 2-component link (Thistlethwaite's 15-crossing one, the EKT families)? (ii) is there any V-trivial 2-component link with |T| == 5, 7, 11 mod 12? A uniform answer "== 1 mod 12" would be a new invariant-level pattern; it cannot be proved by evaluations alone (the unlink has T = 0), so it would be of the same depth as the torsion conjecture, but it would sharpen the search: the first counterexample to T has |T| = 0, far from the only observed residue.

## Correction (05:05) to Proposition C / Corollary D: connectivity types

Redoing the bracket check with the writhe tracked honestly: the crossing's sign in K_+ versus K_- depends on how K is oriented through X, which depends on the connectivity type of X. Write [X] = a id + b e (plat frame). The two arcs of the complement X of a crossing in a KNOT pair the four endpoints either as top-top/bottom-bottom (e-type) or as left-left/right-right (id-type); the crossed pairing gives a 2-component link. Then:

    e-type X:  V(K_+) = V(K_-)  <=>  a = 0;      id-type X:  V(K_+) = V(K_-)  <=>  b = 0.

(The coefficient of the basis tangle of the OPPOSITE connectivity must vanish. Checks: X = e and X = id both close to kinked unknots on both sides; nugatory crossings are e-type with a = 0.) Moreover an id-type X always has a != 0, since N(X) is then a knot and <N(X)> = a + b delta cannot be divisible by delta (det != 0 at A = e^{i pi/4}). So the zeros of the id-coefficient are exactly the e-type V-preserving crossings.

Corrected Proposition C. KL(2m,2) <=> every V-preserving E-TYPE crossing change in an (m+1)-bridge plat diagram is nugatory. The id-type case is the e-coefficient Key Lemma ("b = 0 => split"), equivalently KL for the 90-degree rotated tangle, whose plat width is a different invariant; it is NOT covered.

Corrected Corollary D (conditional on BBB). Every V-preserving e-type crossing change in a 3-bridge plat diagram is nugatory. (e-type: following the strand out of the crossing's top-left you return to its top-right.) Still, I believe, new; weaker than claimed at 03:35. Lemma Q is unaffected: its tangles are e-type by construction (D(X) = U a knot, N(X) a 2-component link).

## Check (05:25): zeros of the id-coefficient are e-type only (empirical)

With Codex's incidence helper probe.geometry for connectivity, 2500 random (word, cap, cup) triples on six strands: 730 had closed components (skipped); of the two-arc tangles, e-type: 100 zeros / 460; id-type: 0 zeros / 731; crossed: 0 zeros / 579. Consistent with the 05:05 argument (id-type N-closure is a knot, so a + b delta cannot be divisible by delta). My own first traversal code had a bug and reported id-type zeros; the incidence helper is the reliable one.

General Band-Jones form of the Key Lemma (restating KL(2m,2) without the unknot): for an e-type two-arc tangle X, a = 0 iff <N(X)> = delta <D(X)>, i.e. the 2-component link L = N(X) has the bracket of K (+) U where K = D(X) is the result of the band move. So KL(2m,2) says: if a band move on an (m+1)-bridge-plat 2-component link L (band in outer plat position) yields a knot K with V(L) = V(K (+) U), then L is split. Lemma Q is K = U. Sigma_2(L) = zero-surgery on a knot in Sigma_2(K); the classical evaluations give H_1(Sigma_2(L); Z/p) = H_1(Sigma_2(K); Z/p) (+) Z/p for p = 2, 3.

Codex (032512): the complement of the marked sigma_1 in the 4-plat sigma_2^2 sigma_1 sigma_2^3 is e-type, the two crossing strands come from the same cap, yet the complement is a nonrational tangle (its horizontal smoothing is Hopf # trefoil, double cover with group Z/2 * Z/3), so it has no width-2 plat position. ## Addendum (06:05): adequacy covers almost nothing; define id-adequacy

Random e-type two-arc samples (probe.py, 2500 triples): 100 zeros / 460; of the 360 nonzero id-coefficients, 317 have unit coefficients at both extremal degrees. The sufficient condition "A-adequate diagram with all-A state of id-type, or B-adequate with all-B state of id-type" (which forces a unit extremal term) holds for 9/360 nonzero and 0/100 zeros. So the unit extremal terms are not explained by diagram adequacy. Definition: a (cap, braid, cup) diagram is id-adequate (id-B-adequate) if its id-type Kauffman states of maximal (minimal) degree are unique. Lens L2 restated: KL(6,2) reduces to tangles none of whose diagrams is id-adequate in either sense; whether that class contains a nonsplit two-arc tangle is open. Codex's four controls (032944) all have leading coefficient 1 with a single monomial of P on the top surviving line.

Instrument requested from Codex (033?): Bigelow's pairing in his combinatorial winding-number form on an unaggregated intersection-pair model, calibrated against H and J_hat, then run on the 14-letter witness, the Bigelow and Long-Paton controls and a long split zero.

## Finding (06:25): the extremal term of the id-coefficient is NOT the extremal id-state; lens L2 as stated is dead

State-sum model validated against Codex's probe.coefficient on 103 samples (exact match). Enumerating all Kauffman states for e-type two-arc samples with 7-10 crossings: in 32 of 38 nonzero cases the id-type states of maximal degree cancel EXACTLY (signed count 0), so the true leading term of a sits at a lower degree; only 2 cases had a unique top id-state. All 22 zeros also had cancelling top id-states (8 further zeros had no id-type states at all).

Explanation: a = (<N(X)> - delta <D(X)>)/(1 - delta^2). If the all-A state s_A of the tangle diagram is e-type, the N-closure adds two circles to it and the D-closure one, so <N> and delta<D> have the same top degree and the same leading sign and cancel; the leading term of a is then at least two degrees below and comes from a cancellation pattern, not from a single state. If s_A is id-type, a's top term is s_A itself (and then it is a unit). So "unit extremal coefficient" (observed in ~88% of nonzero cases) is mostly NOT the s_A term. Lens L2 (adequacy of a single top id-state) cannot be the mechanism.

Lens L2' (replacement, to develop): the first few coefficients of <N(X)> and <D(X)> for A-adequate closures are determined by the all-A state graphs (Dasbach-Lin, head and tail of the Jones polynomial); the leading term of a is a combinatorial invariant of the two state graphs of N(X) and D(X) after the forced cancellations. A theorem "this invariant is nonzero for nonsplit X in an adequate plat diagram" would be the Jones-side Key Lemma for the adequate class. Not started.

## Observation (06:45): the unit-leading-term phenomenon is not adequacy

Random plat words are almost never adequate: of 151 nonzero e-type samples, 1 is A-adequate for both closures, 3 for the tangle only; none of the 64 zeros is adequate in any sense. Yet the leading coefficient of the id-coefficient is +-1 in 114 of 116 nonzero cases (both when the all-A state is id-type, 44/44, and when it is e-type and the forced cancellation has occurred, 70/72). So the leading term of a is a robust +-1 for a reason that is neither a single state nor adequacy. Unexplained; worth understanding, because a structural reason for a unit leading term is a proof of the Key Lemma for the class where it holds. Candidate: a is a difference of two closure brackets whose state graphs differ by one vertex merge; the leading term after cancellation may be a "local" count near the merge (the crossings adjacent to the two arcs' junction), hence +-1 generically and 0 only in degenerate patterns. The degenerate patterns would be exactly the zeros, split or not. This is lens L2'' and is the Jones-side mirror of Codex's "single monomial on the top surviving line".

## Codex results (035541) and a structure lemma at width 6 (07:15)

Codex: (1) U5-LAW-GEO: the canonical geometric pairing is Phi(C,C') = <S_C, j(z_{C'})>' with S_C the puncture-relative SQUARE of the caps and z_{C'} the absolute TORUS of figure-eights around the cups; Phi = H/(1-q)^2, Phi|line = q^{-1} J_hat, Phi(q,1) = q^{-1} det(M_cap). An embedded noodle square cannot be the dual (permanent bound 2 versus delta^2-type values 4). (2) Three e-type completions of the Long-Paton Burau-zero pair: det M = 0, J != 0, nonsplit: the exchange variable revives what Burau kills. (3) In one of them the top line cancels and the surviving unit coefficient is -11+10, at least 21 configuration pairs: a unit coefficient does not mean one pair. (4) The total one-point intersection complexity is REFUTED: the trivial split e-tangle with D=(23)(56) against C0 has minimal total count 2 but zero configuration pairs. (5) The 288 Property R certificates are unaffected by the helper bug. (6) The 14-letter partner map is still not geometric; the PL enumerator failed calibration.

Structure lemma (Claude). Let X = cap_C cup_{C'} be an e-type two-arc tangle at width 6 (2 caps, 2 cups, no closed components). Each arc alternates caps and cups; an e-type arc from a top-free point to a top-free point contains one more cup than cap, so it has (1 cup, 0 caps) or (2 cups, 1 cap); the other arc then has (1 cap, 0 cups) or (2 caps, 1 cup). Hence EVERY such tangle has an arc that is a single cup joining the two top-free punctures, or a single cap joining the two bottom-free punctures. Call it the short arc s. Split criterion: X is split iff s can be isotoped (rel punctures, modulo slides over the other arc of its own system) to miss the two figure-eights of the opposite system; equivalently iff, in the square-torus model, some representative has no complete matching. (If s is a cup joining the top-free punctures and misses both loops around the caps... more precisely: s disjoint from the opposite system's region forces s to be boundary-parallel, so X is split; conversely a split X has s unwindable.) Codex's refuting example is exactly this: s = (56) straight.

Matching complexity: c''(C,C') := min over representatives of the number of configuration pairs = |c_1 ∩ γ'_1||c_2 ∩ γ'_2| + |c_1 ∩ γ'_2||c_2 ∩ γ'_1|. Split => c'' = 0 (by the lemma). Conjecture: nonsplit => c'' > 0 (both matchings cannot be empty; sketch: if the short arc's loop misses both caps, X is split; the other factor is a four-puncture (Burau_4-type) essential intersection). KL(6,2) then reads: c'' > 0 => the collapsed signed sum over the pairs is nonzero. This replaces the refuted total-intersection proxy and is matching-sensitive as Codex requires.

## Correction (07:30) to Lemma A / M2, after Codex 040022 (node U5-POS)

Codex is right: an ambient isotopy preserving S setwise cannot change |U ∩ S|; a destabilisation moves U across the FIXED sphere, and then phi(B') ∩ S need not be phi(D'). Repaired statement, Lemma A': if an inner cancelling pair has bridge disks contained in B (shadows inside the level disk D, not passing through D'), the destabilisation is supported in B, fixes B' pointwise, and lowers the plat width of X by 2. Same for M2 with the inner disk inside B. When a shadow winds through D' (around d_1 or d_2) the disk meets B' (its shadow crosses every band core tau in D'), and no reduction is claimed. Theorem B weakens accordingly: a minimal-width counterexample has no inner or mixed cancelling pair with disks inside B; Otal does not guarantee the extra condition, so the "outer-perturbed" conclusion is only for pairs of that kind. None of this touches the main line (Phi, KL(6,2), the structure lemma).

## Codex instrument (042759) and the parked-point formula (07:50)

Instrument: exact rational PL braid action, exact windings; 81 Gram entries calibrated over 1,859,630 incidences; the 14-letter witness reduces (by planar isotopies, no slides) to four incidences, Phi_geom = (1-q^-1)(1+qt), partner map 0<->2, 1<->3 with monodromy (1,1) (one winding about puncture 1, one exchange). Unreduced representative: 340,350 incidences, top collapsed line has 4,788 records all with b = 0, so a universal (1,1) involution on an arbitrary representative is false. Long-Paton and the 98-letter split zero have generic-lift lower bounds of 57.5M and 3.77M incidences (not slide-invariant). Node U5-INST TRUSTED.

Identification (Claude): the (1,1) correspondence is the diagonal torus of a cap slide (an arc and its parallel copy; exchanging the two points between them costs one half-exchange and one puncture winding), i.e. Bigelow's Basic-Lemma parallel-copy construction; slide defect = (1+qt) x relative square. Explains split => J = 0 geometrically; nothing for the hard direction.

Parked-point formula (conjectural bookkeeping, sent for verification): within a matching b = 2W + m; by sequential homotopy W = w_0(z') + wind_{z'}(path of z), so on the TL line
    Phi = sum_m (-1)^m sum_{z'} unit(z') q^{a(z')-2w_0(z')} <gamma_{m-bar}, c_first>_{D_6 cup {z'}, weight(z') = q^{-2}}.
I.e. the width-6 Jones pairing is a sum of colored-Burau pairings of ONE arc in a 7-punctured disk, the seventh puncture being the parked second point with inverted weight q^{-2} (the Lawrence fibration F_2(D_6) -> D_6 with fibre D_7, local system t^2 on the extra puncture, specialised). Width 4 has no such point. KL(6,2) is then a Key Lemma for a sum of colored-Burau pairings. Counterexample test posed: C = (34)(56) standard, C' = k((12)(45)) with k a Burau_6 kernel element, so all one-point terms vanish and J is carried by the parked-point weights alone; J = 0 nonsplit would refute KL(6,2).

Note (Codex ACK 043153): in the six-letter nonzero control the top collapsed line has 804 incidences cancelling separately at b = 0 and b = 1, and the surviving coefficient +1 at degree -4 comes from 21 incidences of mixed sign. Two distinct cancellation operations must be kept apart: (1) opposite signs at equal (a,b) before specialisation (bigons), (2) distinct monomials on a collapsed line after specialisation ((1+qt)-packets). Any "surviving corner" criterion must name which support it refers to and predict the line from geometry, not from the computed coefficient.

## Codex 043746: structure lemma proved, matching criterion, parity (08:05)

- Exactly one short arc: with r caps on the top-top arc and s cups on the bottom-bottom arc, r + s = 1. Checked on all 2,959 archived e-type two-arc records (1,487 single bottom cup, 1,472 single top cap). Node U5-SHORT TRUSTED.
- Fixed-representative matching criterion: N_ij >= |wind_{gamma_j}(start c_i) - wind_{gamma_j}(end c_i)|; the non-short row/column has both entries >= 1, so the matching count N_11 N_22 + N_12 N_21 vanishes iff the short row (source-short) or short column (target-short) is (0,0), i.e. iff the figure-eight around the short cap/cup misses both arcs of the other system. Node U5-MATCH OPEN for the topological identification (misses both arcs => splitting disk; split => unwindable within fixed-width slides).
- Parity: b mod 2 = matching XOR base matching; the LP surviving line -11 s^27 - 10 s^29 t is -11 (one matching) + 10 (the other): the unit coefficient is a cancellation BETWEEN matchings, so no positivity between them.
- Five archived split zeros have positive matching counts in their saved representatives (planar isotopies alone reduce the arc commutator to [[1,1],[0,0]]); the 14-letter witness has nonzero generic Phi so only slides can empty it.

Consequence for the parked-point formula (Claude): take the short arc in the target system (as in the LP completions: short top cap (56), loop gamma_short). The two matchings pair gamma_short with c_1 and gamma_other with c_2, or the reverse. Parking the point on gamma_other gives
    J = sum_{z' in c_2 ∩ gamma_other} u(z') <gamma_short, c_1>_{D_6 ∪ {z'}, q^{-2}}  -  sum_{z' in c_1 ∩ gamma_other} u(z') <gamma_short, c_2>_{D_6 ∪ {z'}, q^{-2}},
a signed sum of colored-Burau pairings of ONE loop (the short loop) against the source arcs. KL(6,2) in this form: if that sum vanishes, gamma_short can be isotoped (mod slides) off both source arcs. The t = 1 shadow is det M = 0 with the short column, which Burau_6 kernels defeat; the parked weight is the only new ingredient.

## Codex 051309: P1 refuted; the moment ladder does not work fiberwise (09:00)

On the saved six-letter nonzero control all 23 matching-1 parked positions have certified mixed signs at the extremum of their lowest nonzero moment; signs at the other 24 positions remain undetermined. Proposed exchange-symmetry explanation (Claude 051742; geometric statement OPEN): a slide defect might admit a diagonal-torus model with exchange partners. Codex 051758 qualification: writing Phi=F0(q,u)+t F1(q,u), u=t^2, divisibility by (1+qt) is equivalent to F1-qF0 being divisible by (1-q^2u), not to F1=qF0 identically. For Phi=(1+qt)(G0+tG1), F0=G0+quG1 and F1=qG0+G1. The stronger identity needs a separately proved local normal form and compatible lifts. Polynomial decomposition into monomial multiples of (1+qt) is automatic and does not realize a cycle as geometric slide defects. The hard direction remains nonsplit => Phi not divisible by (1+qt), equivalent to KL(6,2); scalar divisibility => realizable geometric defects is an OPEN proposed mechanism. ## Morning (09:05, wall clock): the wrapping-two reduction, and the three-probe theorem read

Clock note: labels above from 06:05 onward ran up to four hours ahead of the wall clock; everything labelled 07:15 to 09:15 happened between about 04:20 and 05:20. From here labels are wall-clock.

Audit (full text in STATUS.md): KL(6,2) is a difficult proposed faithfulness-strength route. The claimed implication to faithfulness up to centre still needs the split-partner separation and action-kernel lemmas identified in Codex reply 090349; it is not established merely by naming splitness. The jet and parked-point statements are a correct dictionary (Jackson-Kerler 0912.2114 non-split extension; Lawrence fibration), with no novelty claim; KL(4,2) rests on Bharathram-Birman-Brendle arXiv:2607.05283. Method from here: theory letters, no computational assignments.

### A. The encircled-pattern form (letter 20261103-084321, theorem in letter 20261103-090406)

Setting, corrected by Codex 092620: N = K_1 cup K_2 with K_1 ASSUMED unknotted and lk = 0. Unlink Jones gives lk = 0 and determinant zero; it does not force a component to be unknotted. In the width-six application the unknotted component must come from the short-arc geometry. V = S^3 - N(K_1) solid torus, P(A,z) = bracket of K_2 in V (z = core), E a spanning disk of K_1 meeting K_2 minimally in w points (wrapping number), B = S^3 - N(E) a ball, T = K_2 cap B a w-string tangle.

Lemma 1 (encircling identity; Snape 2018, Theorem 3.3.14, not a new project result). <K_1 cup K_2> = delta * P(A, z = -(A^4 + A^{-4})) and <K_2> = P(A, z = -(A^2 + A^{-2})). Proof: <U cup e_k> = Delta_k lambda_k, lambda_k = -(A^{2k+2} + A^{-2k-2}), and Delta_k lambda_k = delta S_k(lambda_1) for all k (both equal (-1)^{k+1}[2k+2]); extend linearly. Convention <empty> = 1.

Wrapping two. lk = 0 makes T e-type; with <T> = x id + y e (id = the strand pair whose annular closure is the 0-framed two-cable, e = the turnbacks):
    P = x z^2 + y delta,  <N> = delta(x lambda_1^2 + y delta),  <K_2> = delta(x delta + y),  <D(T)> = delta(x + y delta),
D(T) = the two strings of T reclosed inside E (a two-component link). lambda_1 = 2 - delta^2.

Theorem 2 (Jones-trivial line, corrected and TRUSTED on the actual wrapping-two subclass). V(N) = V(U_2) iff x(delta^2-2)^2 + y delta = u delta, where u is the actual framing/writhe correction. Hence: (i) delta | x, equivalently det D(T) = 0; (ii) x = delta x', y = u - x'(delta^2-2)^2: the locus is one line in the rank-two skein module; (iii) (delta^2-3)<K_2>/delta - (delta^2-4)<D(T)>/delta^2 = u; (iv) a rational e-type cut gives Jones-trivial N only at the trivial e slope, since det D(T) is the denominator. This covers rational clasp/twist patterns, not arbitrary Whitehead doubles with a knotted companion. Whitehead check: x = 1 - A^{-4}, y = A^2. Whitehead itself is NOT one band from U (see DEAD_ENDS).

With compatible oriented normalizations, the t version of (iii) has a PLUS second term:

    (t-1+t^-1)V(K_2) + (t-2+t^-1)V(D(T))/(t^1/2+t^-1/2) = 1.

The minus sign in letter 090406 fails at T=e. Set s=delta^2, k=<K_2>/delta and d=<D(T)>/delta^2. Then k-u=-(s-1)(s-4)x' and d-u=-(s-1)(s-3)x'. These are shared-defect identities, not a decreasing-complexity argument. D(T) naturally has a band to K_2, not automatically to U.

The asserted width-six-to-wrapping-two reduction is OPEN, and its proposed general bridge-count lemma is REFUTED (Codex 092620). L(24,5) has two unknotted components, lk=0 and a joint (1,2)-bridge presentation after one perturbation, but wrapping at least four in either direction. The published Alexander support has span three in each variable, so McMullen's bound on a punctured spanning disk gives 3<=w-1. Sources and the explicit support are in work/w2_review/geometry.md. This counterexample is not asserted to have unlink Jones or a band to U, so it does not refute a reduction using those extra hypotheses.

The missing geometry is mutual disjointness of the upper and lower axis shadows on the punctured sphere; bridge-disk disjointness within each half-ball does not imply it. IF those two shadows can be chosen mutually disjoint, their Jordan curve has a complementary disk containing at most two other punctures, and lk=0 gives wrapping zero or two. Opposite-sign punctures are endpoints of one bridge in some hemisphere, by the four-cycle of the other component's bridge endpoints. A chosen shadow leaving that disk is not by itself an invariant nonsplitness certificate. No automatic two-level cut-tangle reduction is recorded.

Answers to Q1-Q3 (Codex 092620): both actual components of the standard nonzero EKT opposite-omega orbit are knotted. Their index-two patterns live in auxiliary Hopf-companion tori. Component determinants are |a+8bd| and a with a²-8b²=1; at nonzero iterates both exceed one (work/w2_review/ekt.md). This does not classify arbitrary EKT inputs or decide wrapping-two detection. No descent to D(T) is established; the formulas above expose a common defect and may lose the actual-U band. The specialization x mod delta is abelianized branched-cover/Fox information and does not supply a splitting disk.

### A.1. Actual wrapping two gives a Klein-bottle piece (Codex 092620)

**U5-W2-Q, PROVED under the internal protocol after Claude 093811's independent read; no priority claim (Codex 094509).** If N=U union J admits a spanning disk of U pierced twice by J, and H1(Sigma2 N)=Z, then the cut-tangle cover M has H1(M)=Z+Z/g and det J=g² for an odd positive integer g. No Jones hypothesis is needed for this proposition. A band to U and determinant zero imply its homology hypothesis by the Montesinos trick.

The outside cover Q is the orientable twisted interval bundle over a Klein bottle: the annular twofold cover of the disk, branched at its two interior punctures, has its boundary circles identified by the deck involution because the disk boundary also belongs to the branch locus. Its closed preimage is a Klein bottle. With pi1(Q)=<a,b | aba^-1=b^-1>, the boundary basis is (a²,b); the D slope is b and the J slope is a²b^k. This is also the standard (-2,2) pretzel cover (Ishiwata-Przytycki-Yasuhara, math/0109046, Example 2(a)).

Write H1(M)=Z c+T, t=|T|. The rank-one glued homology forces the D slope lambda to be the rational longitude. If ell=i(lambda) has order g, integral boundary duality gives i(mu)=gc+v. The gluing group is (Zc+Za+T)/<gc+v-2a-k ell,2ell>, whose torsion order is (t/g)gcd(g,2)². Torsion-freeness forces t=g odd, ell generates T, and the J filling has order tg=g². Full proof, including the boundary-index argument and marking, is in work/w2_review/geometry.md. The proposed next object on this subclass is the marked embedding of Q in S3_0(kappa); no classification or Jones exclusion has been proved.

### B. Astra's RB-3PROBE (20261103-084934), independent read (09:04)

Statement: R rational, X any two-string tangle in the same marked ball; two actual nonlongitudinal rational closure equalities plus determinant equality at a third slope force X = R unframed. New step: h(M(alpha)) = |T||ell_M(alpha)| for M = Sigma_2(X), H_1(M) = Z + T; then m = |T| ell_M satisfies m^2 = l^2 on three directions, so (m-l)(m+l) vanishes on three pairwise independent vectors, one factor dies on two of them, m = +-l, primitivity of l gives T = 0 and the disk longitude. Checked from scratch: correct. Finish is the audited LATE-41 nonlongitudinal branch (Walker subtraction, RR Floer simplicity, norm, Lickorish). The standalone homology lemma is now RB-HOM3, PROVED under the internal protocol after Claude's independent read; the full RB-3PROBE remains TRUSTED and novelty is unverified. In Lemma Q, a = 0 and D(X') = U give determinant equality at every slope (since [X'] = b e, b a unit). The tangle cover is E(kappa); its longitudinal filling is S^3_0(kappa). No second actual closure is available, which is exactly why the theorem does not reach Lemma Q.

### C. The crossing-circle link: Lemma Q is unlink detection for Sakuma links (09:20 wall clock)

Setting. K = K_+ with the unknotting crossing in a ball B', K_- = U. Let D_c be the crossing disk in B' (a disk meeting U in the two points d_1, d_2 where U's strands cross the level sphere; e-type means the strands are antiparallel there, so lk(c, U) = 0) and c = boundary of D_c the crossing circle. X = U minus B'. Annular frame: U's strands through B' are the straight tangle id_v, <X> = x id_v + y e; the crossing-frame coefficient a of the note satisfies x = a A^{-1} (half-twist change of basis), so a = 0 iff x = 0.

Lemma 3 (checked, Codex 093845). (i) If a = 0 then every rational closure of X has the bracket of the corresponding closure of e, up to the specified framing unit. The stated turnback twist family K_n = X cup sigma^n has V=1; arbitrary rational closures need not be unknots. (ii) Conversely, let c bound a disk meeting the actual unknot U twice, and assume V(c cup U)=V(U_2). The Jones derivative gives lk(c,U)=0, so the cut tangle is e-type and P=x z^2+y delta. In compatible diagrams c has no self crossings and the mixed signs sum to zero, giving the SAME writhe unit u in <U>=delta(x delta+y)=u delta and <c cup U>=delta[x(delta²-2)²+y delta]=u delta². Elimination gives x(delta²-1)(delta²-4)=0, so x=0. The separate-unit proof is also valid: evaluation and first derivative at A=1 force u_0=u_1. This proves the annular equivalence for the branch-set annular knot; no reverse implication for the possibly higher-wrapping longitude annular knot has been established.

Theorem 4 (U5-SAK equivalence, PROVED under the internal protocol after Codex 093845). Lemma Q holds iff: for every unknot U and circle c bounding a disk meeting U in two points, V(c cup U)=V(U_2) implies c cup U is split. Direct proof: Q makes the cut tangle with bracket u e and actual U closure rational, hence e and split. Conversely put any rational-bracket/actual-U tangle into these coordinates (the rational twin's disk slope is distance one from the actual-U slope), and apply crossing-circle detection. Splitness places c in a ball off U. Its two lifts in Sigma2(U)=S3 are then unknots in disjoint balls; the lifted crossing disk is an annulus, whose core kappa is parallel to either boundary. Thus kappa=U, the tangle cover is a solid torus, and the tangle is rational. A nonsplit Jones-trivial crossing-circle link would PRODUCE a knot counterexample by a single full twist and the unknot's nugatory-crossing theorem. The detection assertion remains OPEN; this is an equivalence, not a proof of detection.

The Whitehead link is a crossing-circle link of the unknot (symmetric, wrapping (2,2)); its twist family is the twist knots; it lies in this locus and not in the band-move locus (Astra 090349: Sigma_2 = L(8,3) is not an integral surgery on an S^3 knot). It is the right test case in this role: V(W) != V(U_2) is the statement that no twist knot is Jones-trivial.

Corollary 5 (checked with framing qualification). In Sigma_2(U)=S^3 the crossing disk lifts to an annulus with core kappa and two parallel boundary curves exchanged by the inversion. Flatness gives det D(T)=0, identifying the annulus/Watson framing with the Seifert longitude. Linking number zero alone would only give even Sakuma framing, not framing zero (Snape §2.1.2). Thus the Jones-trivial crossing-circle link is a preferred Sakuma link. Keep N0=D(T), whose cover is S3_0(kappa) and which has an actual band to U, separate from S=c cup U, whose cover is E(kappa) glued to the fixed Klein-bottle interval bundle Q and which has two unknotted components and wrapping two. Neither the band nor the wrapping property transfers automatically between these two links. On the Sakuma link the odd-square test of A.1 has J=U and g=1 already, so gives no additional obstruction.

Sphere picture, qualified by Codex 093845. The stated standard-position construction gives an arc alpha* on the eight-punctured sphere, whose lift lies on the genus-three Heegaard surface. The relevant motion group is the hyperelliptic Goeritz subgroup with the required endpoint stabilizer, not automatically the full Powell-generated group. The stronger statement that unknotness puts this particular marked surface arc in the standard fixed-genus orbit requires a relative-position theorem. It is not established by Otal's uniqueness of the axis presentation. No equivalence with that stronger orbit statement is banked.

### D. Watson's maps and the limits of the surface conditions (Codex 093845)

The complete marked rational-closure Jones family is exactly relative-bracket data: all closures are linear functionals on the rank-two skein module, and the two standard closure functionals have determinant 1-delta² != 0. It cannot give an independent test on the flat fiber. Watson's inverse-limit quotient retains maps absent from these Euler data. For indices i,j outside its eventual injective/surjective transition regions, kappa is the image of the finite composite A_j -> A_i. Its vanishing is a precise sufficient endpoint for detection, but flat bracket => vanishing composite remains OPEN with no mechanism.

A formal obstruction makes the missing structure explicit. Add a nonzero zero-Euler complex H to every object of the rational inverse system, using id_H on every transition. Every Euler polynomial is unchanged; each resolution cone is unchanged up to an acyclic Cone(id_H); but kappa acquires H. This is not asserted to be a classical tangle. It rules out an inference using only those algebraic data, leaving a realizability or geometric-map restriction necessary. Source, stabilization argument and full model: work/sakuma_review/watson.md, Watson arXiv:1311.1085.

The proposed genus-three hyperelliptic/zero-framing/a2 shadow likewise does not detect U. The two-bridge knot b(31,13), up to mirror b(31,18), has Conway 1-2z^4 and determinant31. Any tunnel-one knot has a zero-framed Seifert longitude representative on the boundary of its genus-two tunnel handlebody. The genus-two hyperelliptic involution preserves the nonseparating curve class and reverses an invariant representative with two fixed points. Equivariant stabilization away from it gives genus three. This is not an assertion of the specific width-six cap position or standard marked-arc orbit. Full hand proof and primary group references: work/sakuma_review/heegaard.md. The exact relative-position information must remain an additional hypothesis; the scalar framing/a2 conditions cannot replace it.

Correction (09:38 wall clock, Codex 092620): the claim in section A that width 6 plus nonsplit gives wrapping number two for the band-move link N is FALSE (the two axis shadows need not be disjoint; L(24,5) has wrapping at least four in a joint (1,2)-bridge position). Section C does not use it: there the disk D_c meets U in two points by construction. Also: sign in the t-form of Theorem 2(iii) is plus; the encircling identity (Lemma 1) is Snape's Theorem 3.3.14. Codex's Klein-bottle proposition U5-W2-Q (for N = U cup J with a spanning disk of U meeting J twice and H_1(Sigma_2 N) = Z: H_1 of the cut-tangle cover is Z + Z/g, det J = g^2, g odd; the cover of the neighbourhood of the disk is the twisted I-bundle Q over the Klein bottle with the turnback slope glued to the Klein bottle core b) read and confirmed by Claude; the same Q gives Sigma_2(Sakuma link) = E(kappa) cup Q with lambda to b, H_1 = Z, and Sigma_2(K_n) = S^3_{1/n}(kappa) for the twist family.

Watson (arXiv:1311.1085, Def 10, Prop 11, Cor 15, Thm 1): kappa is the image of the middle composite f_i ... f_{j-1}: A_j -> A_i of the inverse system A_n = Kh~(T(n)) of rational closures; kappa = 0 detects the trivial inversion. Codex: the whole family of rational-closure Jones polynomials carries exactly the rank-two relative bracket (two closures recover the vector; pairing determinant 1 - delta^2), so no invariant built from closure polynomials can add a test on the flat fibre; and a formal countermodel (add a fixed complex H with zero Euler characteristic and identity maps) shows Euler characteristics plus formal skein triangles cannot force kappa = 0. The motion group is the hyperelliptic Goeritz subgroup (Hirose-Iguchi-Kin-Koda arXiv:2004.03098 relate its quotient to the bridge Goeritz group); "unknot => standard marked-arc orbit at fixed genus" still needs a relative-position theorem.

### E. Literature sweep results (09:47 wall clock; five bounded readers, transcripts in the session's workflow journal wf_81ef8d26-c51)

- Jones-trivial links: Stoimenow (J. reine angew. Math. 657, 2011, arXiv:math/0606255) Thm 4.1: no nontrivial semiadequate link has trivial Jones polynomial, even up to units; Cor 4.1: no Montesinos link; Cor 6.3: no 3-braid link. Crossing-number verifications for knots to 24 (Tuzun-Sikora). Unlink detection among 3-bridge links: open; Montesinos and closed 3-braid subclasses settled. All published Jones-trivial 2-component links are EKT's H(T,U): Hopf-link satellites with both components patterned (each of wrapping two in its own torus) and both components nontrivial 2-bridge knots (Thistlethwaite's 15-crossing link = H(5/2,-3) = figure-eight cup trefoil, lk 0). No Jones-trivial link with an unknotted wrapping-two component is on record; existence appears open (consistent with Snape's question).
- Encircling identity: Lickorish GTM 175 Lemma 14.2 / Kirby-Melvin; EKT Prop 2.1' is the closest published matrix form; Snape Thm 3.3.14 the annular form.
- Sakuma's link confirmed: O = p(Fix tau), L = p(l) with l a preferred longitude disjoint from tau(l); both unknotted, lk 0 (Sakuma Prop 3.1.1); on the quotient theta-curve L is two parallel strands along alpha closed by clasps around O, i.e. our crossing circle c. Not the band-move link. Nonsplit for nontrivial inversions (splitting ball lifts to two balls containing l and tau(l), making the 0-framed 2-cable split). eta = Kojima-Yamasaki self-linking of L in the infinite cyclic cover of S^3 - O; equivariant concordance homomorphism; vanishes for amphicheiral unique-inversion knots and some non-slice examples (8_20, both inversions of 8_9), so eta does not detect.
- Related frames: Kotelskiy-Watson-Zibrowius arXiv:2104.13592 (bijection between nontrivial (K,h) and 4-ended tangles with tau(infinity) unknotted, tau(0) the Seifert-longitude surgery; Khovanov multicurves of quotient tangles); KLMWZ (Kh and BN detect whether a Conway tangle is split; equivariant cosmetic surgery); Lobb-Watson GT 2021 (axis-filtered refinement); Boyle-Issa butterfly links (band move upstairs along a half-axis); Di Prisa.
- Band moves in Khovanov theory: Alishahi (h-torsion order bounds unknotting number; crossing-change maps compose to h), Alishahi-Dowlin (u(K) <= 2 nontrivial => Lee sequence collapses at E_2), Sarkar (ribbon distance), Zhuang; Batson-Seed, Lipshitz-Sarkar (split detection needs the F_2[X]/(X^2) module structure, invisible to Jones), Xie-Zhang (rank Kh >= 2^n, equality only for Hopf sums). No statement about one-band-move links.

### F. Toward the marked quotient of the pilot family (10:03 wall clock)

Astra 095051: U4 (flatness => a_2(kappa) = 0) already excludes torus knots, genus-one knots with nontrivial Alexander polynomial, and knots with an integer L-space surgery (U5-A2SUB). Residual class: a_2 = 0, genus >= 2. Pilot 2-bridge family K_n = C(2,-4n,2,2n), fraction (32n^2-1)/(16n^2+2n), Conway 1 - 2n^2 z^4, genus 2; n = 1 is b(31,13) = 31/18.

Principle P (Claude, letter 100120; not implied by U4): a = 0 forces V(K_m) = 1 for every crossing-circle closure and V(N) = V(U_2); so one nontrivial semiadequate, adequate, alternating, Montesinos or 3-braid closure kills flatness (Stoimenow 2011). Torus knots via this route = calibration only.

Sakuma's template, from Di Prisa-Framba arXiv:2303.08794 Sec. 2.3 and Snape Prop 1.2.11: write p/q = [a_1,...,a_n] with all a_i and n even; the two inversions of a hyperbolic 2-bridge knot are I_1(a_1,a_3,...,a_{n-1}; a_2/2, a_4/2, ..., a_n/2) and I_1(-a_n,-a_{n-2},...,-a_2; -a_{n-1}/2,...,-a_1/2), where I_1(alpha; c) has fraction [alpha_1, -2c_1, ..., alpha_m, -2c_m] and the inversion is the pi-rotation about the vertical axis, which runs through the alpha-boxes (even twists, swapped strands, the axis between the two strands: these become Sakuma's coils in the quotient) while each c-box appears twice, mirrored across the axis (one copy survives in the quotient as a self-twist of the arc alpha); the two fixed points are the top and bottom crossings of the axis (the theta-vertices, the clasps of the Sakuma link). For the pilot: [2,-4n,2,2n] gives the inversions I_1(2,2; 2n,-n) and I_1(-2n,4n; -1,-1). The Watson tangle is the theta-curve with the edge alpha drilled out: X = (S^3 - N(alpha), U - N(alpha)); drawing it means straightening alpha, which turns each coil into a full twist of U's arcs around alpha and each c-box into a loop of U around the straightened alpha. Construction pending Astra's description of the figure (letter 100120).

### G. Codex U5-FROB5 (10:15 wall clock): a Frobenius congruence on the covering knot

Statement (Codex 101151, TRUSTED pending Claude's read): if the preferred Sakuma link S = U cup c of (kappa, tau) has V_S = V(U_2), then J_3(kappa) - 1 is divisible by (q+1)^4 Phi_3(q) Phi_5(q) in F_2[q^{+-1}], hence V_kappa(q) = 1 in F_2[q^{+-1}]/(Phi_5) = F_16 (condition (A)). Proof: P(A,z) = annular bracket of c in E(U) (the longitude annular knot, even in z); two measurements P(A,delta) = <c> = delta (c unknotted, zero-framed) and P(A,lambda) = <U cup c>/delta = delta (encircling identity, all units 1 since lk = 0 and both components drawn with writhe 0) give P - delta = (z^2 - delta^2)(z^2 - lambda^2) R(A, z^2); the 2-fold cyclic cover of E(U) lifts c to the zero-framed preferred 2-parallel of kappa, and the periodic state-sum congruence (M. Zhang AGT 2018 Cor 5) gives <lift>(A) = P(A^2, delta(A)) mod 2 (noninvariant states cancel, crossings double, contractible circles lift twice, essential ones once, delta(A)^2 = delta(A^2) in char 2); the framing is checked by linking transfer; <C_2^0(kappa)> = 1 + (delta^2 - 1) J_3(kappa) (z^2 = e_2 + e_0); substituting, with s = delta^2, lambda(A^2) = s^2 mod 2: (s - 1)(J_3 - 1) = s^2(1-s)(1-s^3) R, so delta^4(1 - delta^6) | (J_3 - 1) in F_2[A^{+-1}], which descends to F_2[q^{+-1}] (free module, basis 1, A, A^2, A^3) with delta^4 = q^{-2}(q+1)^4 and 1 - delta^6 = q^{-3} Phi_3 Phi_5 mod 2; Habiro's expansion mod Phi_5 (q^3 + q^{-3} = q^2 + q^{-2}) gives J_3 = J_2 = V mod Phi_5 integrally. Not implied by a_2 = 0 (3_1 # 4_1 has a_2 = 0 and V = 0 in F_16); the trefoil fails (V - 1 = q^2). The (q+1)^4 factor is weaker than Habiro's universal (q+1)^6; Phi_3 independence unassessed. Also: Boyle-Spyropoulos full paper arXiv:2609.40122 (30 Sep 2026); their breadth-vs-local-crossing inequality is still their Conjecture 1.1(2).

Claude's read of U5-FROB5 (10:21 wall clock): correct. Checked: the two measurements and their units (c and U drawn with writhe zero, lk = 0); evenness of P in z (winding zero); the factorisation (B) by two successive exact divisions in Z[A^+-1][y]; the periodic congruence (C) as a state-sum argument (non-invariant states pair off mod 2; invariant states are lifts; crossings double, contractible circles lift to two, essential circles to one, and delta(A)^2 = delta(A^2) mod 2); the framing transfer; z^2 = e_2 + e_0 giving <C_2^0> = 1 + (delta^2 - 1) J_3; the substitution with lambda(A^2) = delta(A)^4 mod 2; descent from F_2[A] to F_2[q] by freeness; Habiro's expansion mod Phi_5 (q^3 + q^{-3} = q^2 + q^{-2}, second N = 3 factor vanishes); Phi_5 irreducible over F_2 since 2 has order 4 mod 5. Sanity computation (scratchpad frob5/frob5_check.py, dependency-free Kauffman bracket of 2-bridge knots from Conway continued fractions, conventions validated on 3_1, 4_1, 5_1, 5_2, 6_1, 7_4 against the standard Jones polynomials and determinants): trefoil V - 1 = q^2 and figure-eight V = 0 in F_16 as Codex states. Pilot family C(2,-4n,2,2n): (A) FAILS for n = 1, 2, 3, 4 (all excluded from flatness by FROB5 alone, both inversions) and HOLDS for n = 5 (det 799); pattern for larger n below. Sweep of 574 two-bridge knots (continued fractions of length <= 4, entries in [-5,5]) up to mirror: 94 satisfy (A), about one in six. So FROB5 is a real sieve on the covering knot, independent of a_2 = 0, and the residual class for a counterexample is {kappa : a_2(kappa) = 0, V_kappa = 1 in F_16, genus >= 2 (two-bridge case), no integer L-space surgery}.

Observation (Claude): x need not be read from Watson's tangle. With U = X cup id and N = X cup e: <U> = delta(x delta + y) = delta u, <N> = delta(x + y delta), so x = (<N>/delta - u delta)/(1 - delta^2), and N is drawn directly from the quotient theta-curve by doubling the edge alpha into a band whose framing is fixed by det N = 0. For the I_1 template (figure read today) the quotient theta-curve is explicit: U the axis; alpha runs from the top fixed point through the single surviving -c_2 box (upper strand), the single -c_1 box (upper strand), then alpha_1 full coils around U, back through -c_1 (lower strand), alpha_2 full coils around U, through -c_2 (lower strand), to the bottom fixed point (traversal order depends on the parities of c_1, c_2; the boxes visited are the same). Each alpha_j box becomes alpha_j full coils of alpha around U because the half-turn doubles the angular coordinate.

Addendum (10:24 wall clock, Codex 102300, read and confirmed by Claude): universal fifth-root identity. In F = F_2[q]/(Phi_5) = F_16 put A = q (so A^{-4} = q) and d = delta(A) = q^2 + q^3, with d^2 + d + 1 = 0. For EVERY preferred Sakuma link S of (kappa, tau): V_S(q)^2 = 1 + d V_kappa(q), because lambda(A^2) = delta(A)^4 = d = delta(A) makes the periodic transfer land on the encircling evaluation point: P(A^2, d) = V_S(q^2) = V_S(q)^2, while the two-parallel side is 1 + d J_3 = 1 + d V_kappa. So this Sakuma evaluation forgets the inversion; V_S(q) = d iff V_kappa(q) = 1 (the flat obstruction is the corollary); V_S = 1 + d^2 V_kappa^8. Satellite limit (U5-FROB5-SAT): B(P(K)) = a_P + b_P V_K(q) in F for every framed annular pattern P (quantum dimension [N] = 0 for N = 0 mod 5, J_N = 1 for N = +-1, J_N = V for N = +-2 by Habiro), so fixed-root satellite amplification cannot add information. Consequence noted by Claude: the residual set n = 0 mod 5 in the pilot family is the twist-eigenvalue periodicity at A = q; every twisting family keeps a density-1/5 residual set under this test. Higher cyclic covers lift the crossing disk to planar surfaces with graph spines (theta_p), parked.

### H. Outside-the-box round (11:56 wall clock), on Caleb's themes

Caleb (11:50): dynamical approaches; entropy or energy functionals; perturb the knots, make them fill space, duplicate, move around; show systems equivalent to other systems; projection analysis. Two workflows launched (8 cross-field routes: circle graphs, symplectic/categorified chi-rigidity, Habiro H_1, arithmetic rigidity, statistical mechanics incl. Boyle-Spyropoulos, braid representations/4-braids, gauge theory/SU(2), minimal-counterexample structure; 6 theme routes: projection averaging, energy functionals, dynamics/entropy, system equivalences, perturbation/duplication, Fourier/spectral), each builder followed by an adversarial referee applying the global-only principle, then a synthesis.

Claude's own hooks, recorded before the reports: (1) Parseval: (1/2pi) int |V(e^{i theta})|^2 = sum of squared coefficients, = 1 iff V = 1, else >= 2, and |V|^2 = V_{K # K*} on the circle is a nonnegative trigonometric polynomial; T = "Jones L^2-energy of a nontrivial knot is at least 2". (2) Projection averaging (Fary-Milnor, Freedman-He): a Jones-trivial knot has vanishing Lando Euler characteristic at both ends in every projection direction; a direction-averaged extreme-state statistic that is an integral-geometric invariant, nonzero for nontrivial curves, would prove T; obstacle: sign cancellation across directions, and configuration-space integrals compute finite-type invariants (forbidden by G1), so the statistic must see the exponent range. (3) Dynamics: twist families and braid powers give linear recurrences (Newton polygon / Skolem-Mahler-Lech); Jones-trivial members form finite unions of arithmetic progressions; an infinite progression forces root-of-unity eigenvalues on the support; the design decision is the choice of family through a single knot. (4) Structural dichotomy: a minimal Jones-trivial knot either has a V-preserving crossing (then an infinite Jones-trivial twist family exists) or every crossing is Jones-rigid. (5) Equivalent systems: what forbids a nontrivial knot from looking like the unknot at every level at once in the Potts/anyon/Chern-Simons pictures (unitarity bound |V(zeta_r)| <= d^{n-1}, reflection positivity, vacuum uniqueness).

Independent pass on Caleb's themes (12:01 wall clock; the six-theme batch was stopped before producing output, at Caleb's request; the eight-route batch continues).

(a) Energy functionals: DEAD as a direct tool. Geometric energies (Mobius, ropelength, total curvature) bound crossing number from ABOVE (Freedman-He-Wang: average crossing number <= (11/12pi)E + 1/pi), hence bound Jones span from above; T needs a lower bound on span for nontrivial knots. No geometric quantity can give one: Kanenobu's families K(p,q) with p+q fixed share one Jones polynomial (bounded span) while their crossing number, hence any energy, is unbounded. Parseval (Jones L^2-energy >= 2 iff nontrivial, assuming T) is a reformulation with no mechanism.

(b) Projection averaging: reduces to the diagram-existence statement (D) with no gain. Milnor's total-curvature proof works because the statistic (number of maxima) has a POINTWISE lower bound in every direction (bridge number >= 2); the extreme-state Euler characteristic has no pointwise bound (it is zero in most directions for most knots), so an average cannot certify a nonzero direction, and any average that is a finite-type invariant is excluded by (G1). What survives is (D) itself, already in the eight-route batch.

(c) Dynamics and entropy: no mechanism for a single knot. Entropy and pseudo-Anosov growth act on FAMILIES (braid powers, twist families), where V is a linear recurrence and Jones-trivial members form finite unions of arithmetic progressions (Skolem-Mahler-Lech; Codex's Newton-polygon lemma); the single-knot statement T is untouched. Flows on knot space (energy gradient, tightening) could at most select a canonical diagram, which is again (D).

(d) Perturbation, duplication, moving around: ONE real structural output. By (G6) and minimality: let K be a Jones-trivial nontrivial knot of minimal crossing number and D a minimal diagram. For each crossing c of D, either the whole twist family at c is Jones-trivial (c is flat), in which case the member with the twist region reduced has fewer crossings, is Jones-trivial, hence is the unknot by minimality, so K is the unknot twisted at a flat crossing complement (this is exactly the flat-twist locus: the crossing-circle link is a Jones-trivial Sakuma link, i.e. Snape's question / Lemma Q in its twist form); or c is Jones-rigid (only finitely many members of its twist family are Jones-trivial). Hence T is equivalent to the conjunction of (i) Snape's question (no nontrivial strong inversion has a Jones-trivial Sakuma link) and (ii) no minimal Jones-trivial knot is Jones-rigid at every crossing of a minimal diagram. Duplication (K # K*, cables) adds nothing V-determined beyond |V|^2 on the circle; cables need J_3.

(e) Equivalent systems: language, no theorem. Potts/Tutte, vertex models, Chern-Simons, anyons (unitarity bound |V(zeta_r)| <= d^{n-1}), state surfaces each restate V = 1 at all levels; none has a theorem forbidding a nontrivial object from matching the vacuum at every level at once, and the Freedman-Kitaev-Wang density says single levels cannot. Boyle-Spyropoulos's projective-space Tutte polynomial is the only live instance and is in the eight-route batch.

Verdict: the themes are natural, and (d) yields a genuine reduction worth keeping; the rest do not justify agents. The six-theme batch stays stopped.

### I. Codex P-POS (12:10 wall clock, msg 115808): positivity imports do not descend to S^3

Astra tested three ways to turn global Jones data into a positive geometric quantity and closed each with an explicit gate. (1) Positive surface skeins: on a once-punctured unknotted torus, D = [c] - [u] = [c] + (A^2 + A^{-2}) is Laurent-positive in every ideal triangulation (Bonahon-Wong quantum trace; Cho-Kim-Kim-Oh Thm 1.3) yet evaluates to delta + A^2 + A^{-2} = 0 under inclusion into S^3; both c and u are zero-framed unknots, so a positive surface defect can be an actual knot-minus-unknot defect killed by the compressing disk; no homological sign character repairs this (band sum of two meridians breaks multiplicativity). (2) Doubled quantum state sums: the Turaev-Viro norm of the exterior is eta_r^2 sum_n |J_{K,n}|^2 (Detcherry-Kalfagianni-Yang), and V fixes only the n = 2 summand; the ordinary-only observable X boxtimes X is not fusion-closed, so |sum a_i|^2 != sum |a_i|^2; the chain-mail Fourier row for |V|^2 has signed endpoint coefficients (Barrett-Garcia-Islas-Faria Martins); and |V_{4_1}(e^{2 pi i/10})|^2 - 1 = -1 shows the squared-amplitude defect is not pointwise nonnegative at unitary levels. (3) The coefficient norm E(K) = sum a_j^2 (= 1 iff V = 1) cannot be a count of geometric complexity: V(T(3,q)) = t^{q-1}(1 + t^2 - t^{q+1}) gives E = 3 for all q while the genus is q - 1. (4) The reflection-double Morse complex has quantum-zero homology direct_sum Hom(Kh_red^{i,j}, Kh_red^{i+r,j}), which is nonzero in odd degree whenever adjacent homological groups share a quantum degree; squaring the index does not remove cancellation. Decision: the missing theorem is a knot-dependent positive defect determined by V, surviving ambient compression and off-diagonal recoupling, whose vanishing gives a disk; none of the tested constructions has these properties; positivity pass parked.

### J. Codex 121620 (12:18 wall clock): a finite-power theorem from the dynamics theme, and two concentration results

D-POWER (Codex; read and confirmed by Claude): for every braid beta on n > 1 strands, only finitely many powers beta^k (positive or negative) have closure with Jones polynomial 1. Proof: s_k = delta V(closure(beta^k)) = sum_j Delta_j(delta) Tr(B_j^k) over the Temperley-Lieb cell modules, B = (-A^3)^{-w} rho(beta); s_k is an exponential polynomial in k with nonzero coefficients c_lambda = sum_j m_{j,lambda} Delta_j(delta); infinitely many solutions of s_k = delta give an arithmetic progression (Lech); grouping eigenvalues by lambda^b, the one-dimensional top block W(n,n) with eigenvalue (-1)^w A^{-2w} contributes the degree-n term Delta_n with coefficient exactly 1 in its group, which cannot vanish since delta is transcendental over the algebraic constants; so w = 0 and the mu = 1 group's coefficient has degree n in delta, which cannot equal delta for n > 1. No effective bound on the last exceptional power; nothing about k = 1. Nearest prior art: Berceanu-Nizami Thm 1.7 (one exponent varying). D-MOM: all power Jones polynomials determine the characteristic polynomial of beta on every generic cell module (Hankel rank = number of distinct eigenvalues), not the eigenspaces or the braid.
D-PROJ: for a bounded combinatorial diagram statistic F, inf over embeddings of the direction-average equals inf over diagrams of F (flatten heights by epsilon); adding one curl of each sign kills both potential endpoint coefficients, so the unweighted potential-endpoint average has infimum zero for every knot type. The unweighted projection-averaging proposal is refuted; a weighted, genuinely geometric density would need an identity forced by V = 1, none known.
D-CROSS: for a standard crossing disk (algebraic intersection zero) the full-twist family has V_n = C + D t^{+-2n}, so two equal values make the family constant (the flat-crossing-to-flat-family step, confirmed); U -> 4_1 -> U by two crossing changes has defects +t^{-2} and -t^{-2} after removing the universal factor, so no pointed positive cone; E(T(3,q)) = 3 with unbounded unknotting number, so no strict energy descent.
