Astra's judgment: no complete mechanism for T in these directions; the useful next idea would be a topology-derived law creating additional observations or controlling changes between interfaces.

Correction to section H(d) (12:27 wall clock, Codex 122444, checked by Claude): the split is right, the twist-reduction wording was wrong for isolated crossings (a full twist takes +1 to -1, not to 0). The correct move is the smoothing lemma (D-SMOOTH): if K_+ and K_- differ at one crossing of a knot and V(K_+) = V(K_-) = P, then the one-component smoothing S has V_S = P EXACTLY. Proof: with F = (-A^3)^w P, <D_+> = F and <D_-> = A^{-6} F; solving A B_0 + A^{-1} B_1 = F, A^{-1} B_0 + A B_1 = A^{-6} F gives B_0 = delta (-A^3)^{w-1} P and B_1 = (-A^3)^{w-1} P; at A = 1 these have absolute values 2 and 1, so the knot smoothing is the unit one; V_S = (-A^3)^k P with (-1)^k = 1 from V_S(1) = 1 and k = 0 from V_S'(1) = 0. Repaired split: K crossing-minimal among nontrivial Jones-unit knots, D a minimal diagram; a flat crossing c gives S with V_S = 1 and c(K) - 1 crossings, so S = U actually; the complement tangle X then has rational bracket (u times a crossingless basis tangle, since the two smoothing closures have brackets u and delta u and the closure matrix [[1, delta],[delta, 1]] is invertible) and an actual unknot closure: exactly Lemma Q's hypotheses. Hence, assuming Lemma Q, every crossing of every minimal diagram of a minimal counterexample is rigid, and T iff (Lemma Q and R), where R: if nontrivial Jones-unit knots exist, some crossing-minimal one has a Jones-preserving crossing in a minimal diagram. General fiber observation (Codex): a knot minimizing crossing number in its ENTIRE Jones fiber has no Jones-preserving crossing in a minimal diagram (the smoothing would be a smaller member), so rigidity of minimal representatives is normal for every Jones fiber and is not evidence about the unit fiber. Also from 122444: Q-PRIM, a group-like Kontsevich-type element exp(D) with fundamental weight 1 to all orders but nontrivial colour-three weight, not realised by a knot (Alexander realizability fails); sutured-hierarchy gluing needs one new closure measurement from V = 1, none found. Both parked.

### K. Codex 125930 (13:02 wall clock): twisting about arbitrary disks, unknot probes span TL, and the one-band seed

D-TWIST-FIBER (read and confirmed by Claude): let K_n be K twisted n full times about a disk meeting K in r points with algebraic intersection w, and d = floor(r/2) + 1. Either all K_n share one normalized Jones polynomial, or any fixed Jones polynomial occurs at most d times (d - 1 if |w| <= 1). Constant families occur only for w = 0 or |w| = 1, with annular class u^{-1} delta F e_0 or u^{-1} F e_1. Proof: delta V_{K_n} = u sum_j p_j Delta_j A^{n c_j}, c_j = j(j+2) - 3w^2 (twist eigenvalue (-1)^j A^{j(j+2)}, framing change n w^2); distinct exponents give a Vandermonde-type system with a unique top-degree determinant term (rearrangement inequality); a constant family needs a resonant sector c_j = 0 with u p_j Delta_j = delta F, and integrality at A = -1 ((j+1) | 2) forces j in {0,1}. Tomography: d full Jones polynomials of the family determine the annular class. Prior art for the expansion: Yokota 1991, Champanerkar-Kofman. Consequence in our terms: for a Jones-unit knot and any zero-intersection twisting circle, either at most d twists are Jones-unit or K is skein-invisible to the circle (annular class delta e_0): the flat/rigid dichotomy for arbitrary disks.
F-U-SPAN: TL_n is spanned over Q(A) by zero-framed (n,n)-tangles whose closure is an actual unknot (inverse skein identity along a tree of band sites; each term closes to U by the Seifert algorithm). Consequence: a fixed Q(A)-linear skein operation with uniform framing correction that preserves normalized Jones on all Jones-unit inputs preserves it on all inputs; "it only needs to work on the unit fibre" does not enlarge the space of linear constructions.
M-GLUE: Bleiler-Eudave-Munoz (composite fusion-one ribbon knots have a nontrivial two-bridge summand) plus multiplicativity of determinants: a composite determinant-one ribbon knot has fusion number >= 2; so K # (-K) for a Jones-unit K cannot seed Lobb's one-band family.


### L. Codex 130900 and 130850 (13:20 wall clock): multi-site twisting, the hyperbolic filling test, and a classical consequence

ACK 130850 precision points (recorded): in D-TWIST-FIBER the bound is floor(r/2) when w = 0 (zero is already a channel), so for r = 2 the starting parameter is the only Jones-unit one in the nonconstant case; the count is of integer parameters, not knot types; the zero-exponent coefficient in the Vandermonde step is the combined difference u p_j Delta_j - delta F, which vanishes in the constant case and contradicts the nonconstant hypothesis otherwise.

D-MULTITWIST (Codex; read and confirmed by Claude). Fix s twist boxes (box i: r_i strands, algebraic strand count w_i, n_i full twists) and everything outside. Then D(A) delta V_(K_n)(A^-4) = sum c_(a,b) A^(a + b.n) with slopes b_(i,j) = j(j+2) - 3 w_i^2, even integers (the full twist acts on the j-sector of TL_(r_i) by A^(j(j+2) - 3 r_i), one full twist changes the writhe by w_i^2 - r_i, and the sign (-1)^(w_i^2 - r_i) is +1 because w_i and r_i have the same parity). Theorem: for any target F the fiber {n in Z^s : V_(K_n) = F} is a finite union of integer solution sets {n : M_P n = v_P} of integer linear systems, one per balanced partition P of the monomials of the defect Phi(A,z) = sum c_i A^(a_i) z^(b_i) (blocks with zero coefficient sum; the equations are the within-block exponent equalities; coincident blocks still cancel; conversely every zero induces a balanced partition). Each nonempty piece is a coset n_0 + ker_Z M_P of a saturated sublattice, so there are no proper-index progressions, rays or curves. Claude's check: for s = 1 an exponent equality with b_i different from b_k pins n and with b_i = b_k is vacuous or impossible, so the fiber is a point set or all of Z, recovering D-TWIST-FIBER's dichotomy (without its bound d).
Two-site certificate: for s = 2 an infinite fiber contains a line n_0 + k v (v primitive), and the line lies in the fiber iff Phi has the Laurent-binomial factor z_1^(v_2) z_2^(-v_1) - A^(n_(0,1) v_2 - n_(0,2) v_1). Check: the substitution z_i = A^(n_(0,i)) t^(v_i) kills the binomial; a Laurent polynomial in (A,t) vanishing at t = A^k for all k is identically zero (the t-degrees separate for large k); the kernel of the substitution is the lattice ideal of v-perp = Z(v_2, -v_1), principal here. For s >= 3 the same argument gives a lattice ideal generated by one binomial per generator of v-perp, not principal; nothing new in that. Two crossing circles (r = 2, w = 0 each): V = a + b x + c y + d x y with x = A^(8 n_1), y = A^(8 n_2); a Jones-unit line with both coordinates varying has direction (1,-1) (n_1 + n_2 = m: b = c = 0 and a + d A^(8m) = 1) or (1,1) (n_1 - n_2 = m: a = 1, d = 0 and b A^(8m) + c = 0) unless the whole template is Jones-unit (grouping the slopes 0, v_1, v_2, v_1 + v_2). Prior art: Champanerkar-Kofman 2006 Prop 2.1 (multivariable twist bracket); Kanenobu's constant-sum fibers.

D-TWIST-HYP (Codex; read and confirmed by Claude). For a positive-dimensional Jones-unit component, fill the coordinates constant on it first; let M_active be the exterior of K and the remaining circles. If M_active is finite-volume hyperbolic then, along any direction in the component with every active coordinate nonzero (one exists: the coordinate kernels are lower-rank subgroups, and a lattice is not a finite union of lower-rank subgroups; it can be a finite union of proper full-rank subgroups, so the rank matters, as Codex 132111 noted), all filling slopes go to infinity in length, and the multi-cusp form of Thurston's hyperbolic Dehn surgery theorem (finite exceptional sets per cusp; fillings avoiding all of them are hyperbolic, with volumes strictly below vol(M_active) and converging to it along the line; a monotone subsequence gives infinitely many distinct knot types) gives infinitely many distinct hyperbolic knots with V = 1. Contrapositive: a positive-dimensional component consisting of unknots has non-hyperbolic active exterior. Calibration: two parallel copies of a crossing circle generating a nontrivial twist family K_m, K_0 = U: K(p,q) = K_(p+q), so the diagonal p + q = 0 is all unknots although neither circle is nugatory; the active exterior contains the essential annulus between the circles. Two independently checkable targets for a counterexample (a balanced-support identity and a hyperbolic active augmentation), no example known; the open topology is whether a Jones-unit component must have non-hyperbolic active exterior and which cancellation surface accounts for it.

Claude's consequence, recorded as a classical consistency check and not a route (CT). T implies: for every finite full-twist template the unknot locus {n : K_n = U} is a finite union of affine sublattices of Z^s (under T it is the Jones-unit locus). CT is a statement about solid-torus Dehn fillings of E(K u c_1 u ... u c_s). For a hyperbolic exterior Thurston puts the unknot parameters in finitely many coordinate hyperplanes, each a smaller template; the one-circle base case is Gabai's solid-torus surgery theorem (finitely many parameters, or all of Z when the circle is nugatory for the unknot); the non-hyperbolic pieces are where a proof would live (on Seifert pieces the solid-torus condition is distance one from the fibre on all but one cusp, affine in the free parameter, and induced slopes across JSJ tori are affine). Expected to be a theorem of Dehn-filling theory, hence a consistency check for T rather than evidence; a template whose unknot locus is not such a union would refute T. OPEN, parked, nobody on it.

### M. Rigid-case agent (13:20 wall clock; bounded workflow wf_899747e8-62c, verdict partial): the unit-fibre crossing formula and the all-crossings obstruction

T-RIGID-FORMULA (agent; checked line by line by Claude). Conventions: <crossing> = A<A-smoothing> + A^-1 <B-smoothing>, V = (-A^3)^(-w) <D> at t = A^-4, the A-smoothing is the oriented one at a positive crossing. Let D be ANY diagram of a Jones-unit knot K, so <D> = (-A^3)^w. For a positive crossing c let L_0(c) be the oriented smoothing (two components, linking number l) and S(c) the one-component smoothing, whose writhe is w - 1 - 4l after reorienting one lobe (self-crossings keep their signs, mixed crossings flip; w = 1 + w_1 + w_2 + 2l). Then exactly
  V(K_-(c)) = t^-1 + t^(3l-1) (t - 1) V(S(c)),
  V(L_0(c)) = -t^(-1/2) - t^(3l + 1/2) V(S(c)),
  P_c := V(K_-(c)) - 1 = t^-1 (t - 1) (t^(3l) V(S(c)) - 1);
negative crossings by mirroring. Check: <L_0> = A^-1 [(-A^3)^w - A^-1 (-A^3)^(w-1-4l) V(S)]; <D_-> = A<S> + A^-1 <L_0> = A^-2 (-A^3)^w + (A - A^-3)(-A^3)^(w-1-4l) V(S); normalizing by (-A^3)^(-(w-2)) gives A^4 + A^(-12l) (1 - A^4) V(S); and V(L_0) = (-A^3)^(-(w-1)) <L_0> = -A^2 - A^(-12l-2) V(S). Consequences: c is flat iff t^(3l) V(S(c)) = 1 iff l = 0 and V(S(c)) = 1 (a monomial knot Jones polynomial is 1 because V - 1 has the factor (t-1)^2): D-SMOOTH with the writhe bookkeeping explicit. In a crossing-minimal diagram of a crossing-minimal counterexample every crossing is therefore Type I (S(c) = U and l nonzero: V(K_-) = t^-1 - t^(3l-1) + t^(3l), V(L_0) = -t^(-1/2) - t^(3l+1/2); Arf forces l = 0 or 3 mod 4, det K_- in {1, 3}; l = -1 is the trefoil/Hopf pattern) or Type II (V(S(c)) not 1, all skein data affine in V(S(c))). So the unit fibre linearizes every skein triple: the n smaller knots S(c) and the integers l_c determine every crossing-change and smoothing polynomial of D. Twist family at c: V_n = 1 + P_c (1 + t^2 + ... + t^(2(n-1))) for one twist direction, mirror for the other, consistent with D-CROSS (F_n = C + D t^(2n), F_0 = 1, F_1 = 1 + P_c); it meets the unit fibre only at n = 0 when P_c is nonzero. Lando remark: the extreme potential coefficients of every diagram of K vanish unless 3w equals the corresponding potential extreme degree, in which case that coefficient is +-1; the extreme coefficients of the twisted diagrams D_n(c) are those of P_c.

T-RIGID-SUM (agent; checked by Claude): the all-crossings obstruction. With B_D(A,d) = sum_s A^(sigma(s)) d^(|s|-1) the state polynomial (<D> = B_D on the curve d = delta), 
  sum_c A <D_A(c)> = ((n + 3w)/2) (-A^3)^w + (A^2 - A^-2) Phi_D,   Phi_D = d/dd B_D on the curve.
Check: sum_c A<D_A(c)> = sum_s a(s) A^(sigma(s)) delta^(|s|-1) with a(s) = (sigma(s) + n)/2, and A d/dA B = A d<D>/dA - A delta'(A) Phi_D = 3w (-A^3)^w + 2 (A^2 - A^-2) Phi_D. So every sum over all crossings of skein data (crossing changes, oriented smoothings, twist families) factors through the single Laurent polynomial Phi_D, the normal derivative of the Tutte-type state polynomial off the Jones curve: a diagram quantity that V = 1 does not constrain and rigid unknot diagrams realize freely. Per-crossing rigidity is an inequation, so no counting argument; Type I gives no lower bound on span V(S(c)), so no degree argument; B_D has nonnegative coefficients and collapses to a monomial on the curve for unknot diagrams too, so positivity cannot see knottedness. The one input separating a rigid Jones-unit diagram from a rigid unknot diagram is knot detection (Kronheimer-Mrowka), which must be imported, not derived.

Lemma T (OPEN; the agent's one first lemma, unverified): a Type I crossing of a minimal Jones-unit knot has l = -1 after mirroring; equivalently no knot has V = t^-1 - t^(3l-1) + t^(3l) and no two-component link of linking number l has V = -t^(-1/2) - t^(3l+1/2) for l outside {0, -1}. Sketch offered: Arf kills l = 1, 2 mod 4; for l = 0, 3 mod 4 use Lee generators of L_0(c) in homological degrees 0 and 2l (rank Kh >= 4 against a binomial Euler characteristic), the Khovanov skein triangle at c, and span V <= c(K_-). If true, Type I crossings are unknot-like and R reduces to statements about the Type II smoothings. Literature noted by the agent: Ganzell 2014 (an auxiliary bracket f with (A^12 - 1) | f(K_1) - f(K_2) for a crossing change), Balm-Friedl-Kalfagianni-Powell 2012 and Lidman-Moore 2017 (cosmetic crossings), Stoimenow 2011 (semiadequate), Gonzalez-Meneses-Manchon-Silvero 2015 (Lando). Verdict recorded: partial; the rigid case is PARKED with no bracket-level handle beyond Lemma T.


### N. Eight-route batch, synthesis (13:31 wall clock; workflow wf_2f8f87e8-37a, 16 agents, 1.85M tokens): no mechanism, one refutation, three corrections, two small class statements

Outcome. Seven builders reported (the Habiro builder died on the output cap, so that route was not built); all seven referees returned "killed"; the synthesizer ranked what survives repair. Nothing on the table has a mechanism for an arbitrary knot. Three walls recur: (i) Kronheimer-Mrowka gives rank and V gives Euler characteristics, and the one column where they were hoped to meet, the extreme Khovanov column, can cancel (GMS Thm 13; 10_132), so the chi-to-rank gap is intrinsic rather than a missing lemma; (ii) for 4-braids the character data IS T_4 (Korzun-Lanina-Sleptsov arXiv:2402.02553 three-character decomposition of the bracket) and the V = 1 fibre is an infinite union of exchange-move orbits (Morton 1983), so any rigidity re-runs Birman-Menasco with V = 1 in place of unknottedness; (iii) every satellite reduction trades J_2 for J_3 (one colour up). A genuine mechanism would have to control the Euler characteristic of a non-extreme Khovanov column, or use several colours at once, which leaves V.

REFUTATION of N2-R (the one-ended diagram-existence statement behind route N2, i.e. (D) in the form that implies T; named N2-R at Codex's request, 134619, to keep it apart from T-SPLIT's R, the flat-crossing condition, which this example does not touch). N2-R: every nontrivial knot has a diagram D and an end E with nonzero extreme coefficient I(G_E(D); -1) and p_E(D) nonzero (p_A(D) = (c_+ - 2c_- - s_A + 1)/2, p_B(D) = (2c_+ - c_- + s_B - 1)/2 the potential extreme degrees in t; Bae-Morton 2003: p_A(D) <= min deg V <= max deg V <= p_B(D), with equality at an end iff the Lando independence complex there has nonzero reduced Euler characteristic). Counterexample (circle-graph referee's connected-sum argument plus the symplectic referee's cancelling end, assembled by the synthesizer, checked by Claude): K = 3_1^(#7) # 10_132 with right-handed trefoils and the chirality of 10_132 whose V is q^-2 + q^-4 - q^-5 + q^-6 - q^-7. Claude's check from the Knot Atlas table of 10_132 (unreduced Kh over Q, this chirality): the top q-row j = -1 has rank 2 (r = 0 and r = -1) with chi = 0, one q-step above the Jones support (chi rows -3, -5, -7, -15). The long exact sequence Khr^(r, j-1) -> Kh^(r, j) -> Khr^(r, j+1) -> Khr^(r+1, j-1) gives Kh^(*, J+1) = Khr^(*, J) at the top reduced degree J, so Khr(10_132; Q) has top column q = -2 of rank 2 and chi = 0, above the Jones top degree -4 (q^2 convention). Kunneth for reduced Khovanov homology over Q under connected sum (Khovanov, Patterns I) then puts the top column of Khr(K) at rank 1^7 x 2 = 2 with chi = (-1)^7 x 0 = 0, while min deg V_K = 7 - 7 = 0. For every diagram D the Khovanov complex of D is supported in q-degrees at most the diagram's potential top degree, and GMS (arXiv:1511.05845, Thm 4.4) identifies the Khovanov group at that potential degree with the Lando independence complex's homology, whose Euler characteristic is the extreme bracket coefficient; so a B-sharp diagram would force the top column of Kh(K) to have nonzero chi. Hence no diagram of K is B-sharp, and every A-sharp diagram has p_A(D) = min deg V_K = 0. N2-R fails for K (a knot with V nonzero, so T is untouched). Codex 134619 reopened the Knot Atlas page and confirmed the table, the exact-sequence step (both adjacent J+2 groups vanish at the top, so no characteristic-zero splitting is assumed) and the Kunneth step; the older two-ended GMS-seed/mirror construction in work/global_design/n2.md is a separate certificate against the broader extreme-diagram assertion. Consequence: the degree-realization route is closed; the extreme column can cancel at an end for EVERY diagram. The only form still implying T is R' (nonzero extreme coefficient at an end with p_E nonzero, or nonzero almost-extreme coefficient at an end with p_E = 0), which is Stoimenow's semiadequate argument (math/0606255) generalized, with the almost-extreme Lando description available for adequate ends only (Przytycki-Silvero 2020) and no non-adequate mechanism. Also noted by the referee: the two-ended version "some diagram has span V = c(D) - g_T(D)" is false (T(5,6)); the degree gaps gamma_A(K) = min deg V_K - max_D p_A(D) >= kappa_A(K) = min deg V_K - (j_min Kh(K) + 1)/2 >= 0 sandwich the Khovanov cancellation and realization gaps (a remark, not a route).

CORRECTIONS (errors in Claude's brief to the builders and in referee folklore; none of them was on the collab board, but two were in the morning handoff summary):
(a) "Jones's degree bounds for braids: (e - n + 1)/2 <= min deg V <= max deg V <= (e + n - 1)/2" is FALSE: 4_1 = closure of (s_1 s_2^-1)^2 has e = 0, n = 3, min deg V = -2 < -1; 3_1 = closure of s_1^3 has max deg 4 > 2. Correct statements: the bracket bounds p_A(D) <= min deg V <= max deg V <= p_B(D) for every diagram; equality min deg V = (e - n + 1)/2 for POSITIVE braids (this is what work/global_design/order_tl.md and order_extreme.md actually prove); the (e -+ (n-1))/2 bounds are Franks-Williams-Morton for the HOMFLY v-degree. Therefore "|e| <= n - 1 for every braid representative of a Jones-trivial knot" and "TB(K), TB(K*) <= -1" are UNPROVEN as stated (they would follow from T via Birman-Menasco, and from the valid bound only in the form e <= n - 1 + 2 c_- for a braid word with c_- negative letters). Every statement that used "V = 1 forces e in (-1, -3)" for 4-braids must run over all odd e.
(b) "Quasipositive knots have min deg V >= 0" (referee folklore S1) is FALSE: 8_20 is quasipositive (up to mirror the closure of s_1^-3 s_2 s_1^3 s_2, two conjugates of s_2 on three strands, g_4 = 0) with V = -t^-5 + t^-4 - t^-3 + 2t^-2 - t^-1 + 2 - t (det 9), min deg -5 or -1 by chirality. A disk braided surface is a slice disk, not an unknotting, so the braided-surface step (S(b)) could at most give slice.
(c) "V_K = 1 forces s(K) = 0" is unjustified: 10_132 and 5_1 share V (up to mirror) with |s| = 2 and 4. Correct Khovanov residual for a Jones-unit knot: the Lee survivor sits at (0, s); every column of Khr(K; Q) has chi = 0 except q = 0 with chi = 1; if s is nonzero the Lee column itself cancels (it contains a second generator of opposite parity).
(d) The statistical-mechanics builder's source "Boyle-Spyropoulos arXiv:2609.40122" could not be located by its referee, but Codex 134619 opened it directly: Keegan Boyle and Dean Spyropoulos, Myopic Tutte polynomials and Khovanov homology in RP^3, arXiv:2609.40122, submitted 30 September 2026, 43 pages; its Conjecture 1.1(2) is the breadth >= beta inequality and the following paragraph states the implication for Jones unknot detection; the alternating results are theorems, the general inequality is conjectural. Citation VERIFIED; the route stays parked (the referee's other points stand: the myopic lemma's premise J^R = V_K holds for one of the two shadings only, and the Potts curve never enters the physical region; work/next_theory/projective.md separates the exact dictionary from the missing noncancellation inequality).

Survivors with repair (ranked by the synthesizer; none is a route to T): (1) R' as above, with the negative-forest Lando lemma as its first lemma: for a knot diagram whose negative crossings N form a forest in the Seifert graph and whose positive crossings between circles of one N-component are parallel to N-edges, s_A = s - |N|, p_A(D) = g_c(D) - |N|, the all-A Lando graph has vertex set N u P with each negative crossing adjacent exactly to its parallels (nested, pairwise non-interlaced), chords of distinct negative crossings never interlace, and parallels y of x and z of x' interlace iff x, x' share a Seifert circle on which the four sites occur in cyclic order (x, x', y, z); hence a negative crossing with no parallel makes the complex a cone (not A-sharp), and no cross-interlacing gives I(G_A) = S^(|N|-1), extreme coefficient +-1, min deg V_K = g_c(D) - c_-(D) (|N| = 1 is Stoimenow JEMS 2005). Referee: nesting claim verified, four-site criterion derived by hand only; a correct-looking small theorem on degree realization, a class theorem beyond semiadequate knots, not a mechanism. (2) Cables: no cable C_(p,q) of an adequate, torus or iterated-cable companion is Jones-trivial, because V of a cable is a Z[A^+-]-combination of coloured Jones polynomials of C at colours up to p + 1 (Morton; Rosso-Jones) whose degrees Kalfagianni-Tran (arXiv:1501.01574) give exactly, so the top colour dominates both ends unless pq is a Jones slope (Lemma C: the two extreme-degree equations at colours p +- 1 are inconsistent for adequate C, degenerate slope to be handled). Probably an immediate corollary of Kalfagianni-Tran's n = 2 formula (to be checked before any claim); the hypotheses are exactly what a Jones-trivial knot's companion escapes. (3) 4-braids: T_4 is the trace identity tr rho_4(beta) = c_e + t Delta_(closure(pi beta)) for 4-cycles beta (KLS decomposition; Burau_4 faithfulness, Bharathram-Birman-Brendle arXiv:2607.05283, closes the Bigelow-Ito kernel source); Przytycki-Silvero arXiv:2305.18648 Cor 1.6 (extreme group of a 4-braid closure is 0, Z or Z[k] + Z^m[i], m <= 3) gives a word-level condition complementary to the trace identity. Value-blind; infinite solution set. (4) A' (representations referee): V(closure beta) = V(unlink_n) forces beta = 1 for n <= 4; never touches knots.

Process note: three errors passed referees (items (a)-(c)); the synthesizer recommends a cross-referee pass before any next round and no further route generation ("freeze route generation and spend the week on the two lemmas"). Caleb's standing instruction is no new batches; the two lemmas are recorded as candidates, nobody is on them.

### O. Codex 132111 (13:31 wall clock): the AJ-symbol stability theorem and what the classical recurrence forgets

Q-AJ-SYMBOL (Codex; read and confirmed by Claude). K = Q(q, M), sigma(M) = qM, R = K[E; sigma] (a principal left ideal domain). For a q-holonomic J and any rational g in K, let F = J - g, P_J and P_F the minimal annihilators, each normalized to minimum coefficient valuation zero at h = q - 1, with bars the nonzero reductions in Q(M)[L]. Then bar(P_F) | (L - 1) bar(P_J) and bar(P_J) | (L - 1) bar(P_F); so every factor other than L - 1 has the same multiplicity in both, and the L - 1 multiplicities differ by at most one. Claude's check of the proof: r = P_J g is in K and P_J F = -r; Q = (E - sigma(r)/r) P_J annihilates F; sigma fixes h and preserves the h-adic valuation ring (sigma(M) = (1 + h) M, a unit multiple), inducing the identity on the residue field Q(M), so sigma(r)/r = sigma(r_0)/r_0 for r = h^k r_0 has valuation zero and reduces to 1; the minimum-coefficient valuation on R is multiplicative because the reduction of the valuation ring of R is the commutative domain Q(M)[L] (products reduce termwise since bar(sigma^i(q_j)) = bar(q_j)); hence bar(Q) = (L - 1) bar(P_J), and Q = R_F P_F with R_F primitive gives the first divisibility, the second by symmetry. Degree in L may drop under reduction; M-only factors are units. TRUSTED; novelty unchecked.
Q-COLOR-SURGERY: in the reduced zero-framed Habiro expansion J_N = sum_k H_k(q) C_(N,k), C_(N,k) = prod_(j=1)^k (q^N + q^-N - q^j - q^-j), each C_(N,k) is a Laurent polynomial in q and M = q^N, so F_N = J_N - sum_(k=1)^r H_k C_(N,k) has F_1 = ... = F_(r+1) = 1, keeps H_k for k > r, and keeps every non-(L - 1) factor of the classical minimal recurrence. Consequence: the nonabelian factors of the recurrence (the A-polynomial side of AJ) cannot by themselves force a finite colour, in particular J_2 = V, to be nontrivial; a proof through AJ needs an actual-image constraint tying the solution and its initial conditions to the knot beyond the classical symbol (strong AJ peripheral ideals, graph-level rationality, geometric realization untested). Warning example: for the pilot b(31,13) (a_2 = 0, Conway 1 - 2z^4, AJ proved by Le-Zhang Cor 1.9) the deletion of H_1 keeps the nonabelian factors and the Alexander diagonal but the result is the coloured sequence of no knot: MMR would force Delta = Delta_pilot, det 31, while |F_2(-1)| = 1; the identity |V(-1)| = |Delta(-1)| rules it out. Dunfield-Garoufalidis Cor 1.4 (math/0405353) makes full-coloured unknot detection conditional on AJ and does not convert J_2 = 1 into all colours 1; both gaps stay. Route Q-PROP unchanged (parked).
Also from 132111: trinomial Jones polynomials beyond the trefoil's exist, V(T(3,q)) = t^(q-1) + t^(q+1) - t^(2q) for q > 1 coprime to 3 (8_19, 10_124, ...), but not of the Type I pattern t^-1 - t^(3l-1) + t^(3l) outside l = -1; Lemma T stays OPEN. CT acknowledged as conditional, not a theorem from the JSJ sketch; no nonlinear unknot-locus template known to Codex.

CT cases (Codex 133056, 13:35 wall clock; read by Claude). CT-RAT (TRUSTED, checked): for entries x_i = L_i(n) integer-affine in a common parameter vector n, the unit-numerator locus of the continued fraction [x_1, ..., x_m] (numerator = first column of prod M(x_i), M(x) = [[x,1],[1,0]]) is a finite union of saturated integer affine flats. Proof by small-entry descent: if all |x_i| >= 2 then |p_k| > |p_(k-1)| and |p_m| >= m + 1, so a unit numerator forces an entry in {0, +-1}; the moves (a, 0, b) -> (a + b) (M(a)M(0)M(b) = M(a + b)) and (a, e, b, c, ...) -> (a + e, -b - e, -c, ...) (M(a)M(e)M(b) = e M(a + e)M(-b - e) diag(-1, 1), the diagonal sign matrix then negating all later entries and changing only column signs) preserve |p| and shorten the list; leaves are the empty list or a singleton +-1; branch conditions are affine equalities in n. So CT holds for two-bridge full-twist templates without Jones (prior art for the unit classification: Kauffman-Lambropoulou math/0212011). CT-HYP2 (TRUSTED, Baker-Motegi arXiv:1705.10373 p. 2 cited for the one-circle input): two disjoint twisting disks with hyperbolic E(K u C_1 u C_2): the unknot locus is a finite union of coordinate lines and points (exceptional coordinate lines; on each, the other circle remains a twisting circle for the filled knot, whose one-circle family is constant for wrapping <= 1 and has at most two unknot parameters otherwise). Does not induct automatically to three disks. CT-SF (checked): a Seifert block (disk with r holes) x S^1 with inner fillings a_i mu_i + b_i f is a solid torus iff all but at most one |a_i| = 1 (a_i = 0 included via lens-space splitting), a finite union of coordinate flats; but the induced outer meridian need not be affine: in the pair-of-pants block with mu_0 + mu_1 + mu_2 = 0, fillings n mu_1 + f and mu_2 + m f give a solid torus with outer meridian n mu_0 - (1 + nm) f, bilinear (Claude recomputed: mu_0 = -(1 + nm) mu_1, f = -n mu_1 in H_1 = Z). Claude's "induced slopes are affine" withdrawn. Homological fact: for disjoint twist circles with linking numbers l_i = lk(K, C_i) and fillings mu_i - n_i lambda_i, the outer primitive homological longitude is lambda_K - (sum_i n_i l_i^2) mu_K, affine; if the exterior is a solid torus this is its disk slope; the affine marking is extra structure to carry through any JSJ calculation but does not itself force the longitude to bound. Claude's remark: in the bilinear example the parameters enter the fibre coefficient multiplied by the exceptional order, so a final distance-one condition is n(affine) = small integer, still flats; multilinear unit equations can have non-flat solution sets in general (n m + k = +-1), so CT's fate rests on whether a JSJ tree can feed a parameter in additively without a multiplier. CT stays OPEN/PARKED; the three restricted results banked; proving CT in general would compare the shapes of two loci, not identify their points, so it is not a route to T.


### P. Codex 134311 (13:47 wall clock): genus-two rung, adjacent mechanism tested and refuted (G2-DENSE, G2-DGW)

Context: the genus-two rung is the faithfulness of W(5,1) / Jones's genus-two representation rho (5-dimensional, via Birman-Hilden from the TL representation of B_6 on W(6,0) with the scalar normalization t^5 = A^-4 that kills the hyperelliptic relation). Codex read the saved g2-proof lane notes without editing them and tested one adjacent idea: recover Morita's homomorphism d_hat on the genus-two Torelli group I_2 from a Dupont-Guichardet-Wigner rotation quasimorphism of SU(2,3) through the Jones image.
G2-DENSE (Codex; read by Claude, TRUSTED modulo the Kasahara citations): with t = -exp(h), Kasahara (math/0012216, Thm 1.1 and the proof of Thm 1.3) gives rho(I_2) = I mod h, first-order terms spanning the 14-dimensional Sp(4)-module Gamma_(0,2) and [Gamma_(0,2), Gamma_(0,2)] = Gamma_(2,0) (10-dimensional, the adjoint): these are the traceless self-adjoint and skew-adjoint parts of sl_5 for the invariant form at the symplectic point (sl_5 = S^2_0(V_5) + Lambda^2(V_5) = 14 + 10 as Sp(4) = SO(5)-modules; checked). For g = I + O(h) in the image, log g lies in the Lie algebra of the Zariski closure H over Q((h)) (every h-coefficient of P(exp(z log g)) is a polynomial in z vanishing at all integers); 14 logarithms with leading terms spanning Gamma_(0,2) and 10 brackets with independent leading terms in Gamma_(2,0) give 24 elements with independent leading terms, so dim Lie(H) >= 24 and H = SL_5 (det rho = 1 on I_2). So the generic Jones image of the genus-two Torelli group is Zariski dense in SL_5; this is a deduction from the first graded piece only, not a faithfulness statement.
Hermitian structure (checked by Claude): in the five cap states of W(6,0), a = (12)(34)(56), b = (12)(36)(45), c = (14)(23)(56), d = (16)(23)(45), e = (16)(25)(34), the Gram matrix G_(C,D) = delta^(loops(C u D)) divided by delta has, at delta = 0 (A_0 = exp(pi i/4)), nonzero off-diagonal entries exactly at the one-loop pairs (a,d), (b,c), (b,e), (c,e) (all other pairs have two loops, the diagonal three): the adjacency matrix of K_2 u K_3, eigenvalues 1, -1, 2, -1, -1, signature (2,3), nondegenerate on a unit-circle interval around A_0; the TL braid operators are unitary for it when |A| = 1 (sigma_i^dagger = A^-1 + A e_i = sigma_i^-1). The separating twist is tau^2 with tau the three-strand full twist (chain relation (T_1 T_2)^6 = T_sep), tau has eigenvalues A^-6 (multiplicity 4, the j = 1 sector, 2 x 2) and A^6 (multiplicity 1) on W(6,0), so rho(T_sep) = t^-9 tau^2 has eigenvalues t^6 (x4) and t^-24 (x1), equal to I at t = -1, consistent with Kasahara's rho(I_2) = I mod h (the symplectic point, where rho factors through Sp(4,Z) on the 5-dimensional representation). Note (Claude): at A_0 the quantum integer [2] = -delta vanishes and TL_3 is not semisimple, so diagonalizability of tau at the endpoint is not automatic from distinct eigenvalues; the endpoint statement rests on Kasahara's theorem rather than on the eigenvalue count. Codex 135015 repaired this directly: the central full twist of TL_3 satisfies (tau - A^-6 I)(tau - A^6 I) = 0 generically (scalar on the two simple summands), the identity holds in the integral diagram algebra and so specializes; at A_0 it reads tau^2 + I = 0 (A_0^6 + A_0^-6 = 0), so with u = t^-9 = -1 the normalized twist u tau^2 is I without any semisimplicity or intertwiner assumption; checked by Claude. A continuous gauge of the forms gives a family rho_s into a fixed SU(2,3) starting at the trivial representation of I_2.
Topological density: at transcendental t on the interval the real Zariski closure of rho_t(I_2) is SU(2,3); a separating twist has infinite order and is elliptic, so the closure of rho_t(I_2) has nonzero Lie algebra, an ideal of the simple su(2,3) by Zariski density, hence rho_t(I_2) is topologically dense in SU(2,3). Every continuous real character of rho_t(I_2) in the matrix topology vanishes; a d_hat factoring through rho would be a discontinuous character (not excluded: dense subgroups have discontinuous characters).
G2-DGW REFUTED (Codex; argument checked by Claude): the path lift s -> rho_s(x) is a homomorphic lift of rho_t to the universal cover of SU(2,3) that kills the generic kernel (constant identity path), so Q_t = Phi o rho_t-tilde descends to the specialized Jones image; but the lifted image is dense in the universal cover (its closure C is normal in a dense subgroup, hence in the cover, with Lie algebra all of su(2,3) by simplicity and nonzero because a central C would contradict dense projection), so if Q_t were additive on I_2 the continuous defect of Phi would vanish on a dense product set hence everywhere, making Phi a continuous homomorphism of a connected semisimple covering group, zero, contradicting Phi(z) = 1 on the central generator (Funar-Pitsch 1209.0302 sections 4 and 5.2 use that central winding). So no homomorphic lift at this specialization makes the rotation quasimorphism a homomorphism; the direct rotation-as-d_hat mechanism is dead. Vanishing of Meyer's cocycle on Torelli does not repair it (that uses the symplectic representation, trivial on I_2). Notes: work/global_design/g2_density_gate.md, g2_quasimorphism.md. No claim about the order of Z_rho, a kernel braid, or Jones detection; the lane's own mechanisms (BBB transfer, Z_rho builders) untouched. Relayed to the structure and breakthrough chats by Claude at Codex's request.


### Q. Genus-two lane consolidated (15:23 wall clock): W(5,1), the BBB gap, Lemma C, finite orders

Sources: ~/jones-lanes/g2-proof/REPORT.md and LETTER-astra.md (Caleb's breakthrough chat, workflow agents with referees), Codex 150127 and 151334 (Codex read the lane files directly, so the lane's letter counts as delivered; archived as msgs/20261103-152335-claude.md). Question: is W(5,1), the five-dimensional Temperley-Lieb module of B_5 (shape (3,2); = Jones's genus-two representation modulo the hyperelliptic involution), faithful? Ladder (published): Ito 2015 needs a kernel of the full TL representation, Long 1986 Lemma 2.1 reduces that to the summands W(n,k); with Bigelow's Burau_5 kernel, W(5,1) unfaithful implies T false; W(5,1) faithful is equivalent to genus-two faithful mod iota and implies Burau_4 faithful (Kasahara 2008 arXiv:0803.0304 Cor 4.1, Thm 5.1, Rem 5.6).

G2-BBB (lane item 8, DAMAGED; Codex 150127 section 4 scoped it; Claude verified the certificate). Bharathram-Birman-Brendle arXiv:2607.05283v2 ("Burau_4 is faithful"): under their parity condition every Moody monomial has the same sign at t = -1 (their eq. (1), W_i = k_(i+1) - k_i), so the count compared in Cor 6.8 equals |M(-1)| (Bigelow's winding lemma: |M(-1)| = number of intersection points), a t = -1 value, blind on the kernel of Burau_4(-1), which contains every Burau_4 kernel element. The Brunnian braid Phi_0 = [(s_1 s_2)^6, [[A_14, A_24], A_34]] is nontrivial and lies in that kernel. Claude's check (scratchpad burau/phi0.py, exact rational arithmetic, unreduced Burau, word length 112): Phi_0 is the identity at t = -1 in B_4 and in B_5 and is not the identity at t = 2 or t = 3 (row one at t = 2: 82415/8192, -39627/4096, -12303/2048, 54243/8192); Brunnian by construction (deleting strand 4 kills the inner commutator, deleting 1, 2 or 3 kills one of A_14, A_24, A_34); triviality at t = -1 also follows from (s_1 s_2)^6 acting trivially on H_1 of the branched double cover (its lift is a twist about a curve homologous to boundary). So the combined package used in Prop 6.7 and Thm 6.9 (preparation of every nontrivial Brunnian input, two-sided parity, different counts) cannot all hold: Phi_0 relates two parity configurations with equal counts. This does NOT isolate Prop 6.7 as false on its conditional proper-product domain, does NOT refute the bare inequality of Cor 6.8, and does NOT refute Burau_4 faithfulness (higher exponent data could still separate equal-count polynomials). Sharpening from Caleb's structure chat (~/jones-lanes/g2-proof/BBB-GAP.md, 15:25 wall clock; three independent skeptics checked it against the paper text and all returned 'survives' at 15:30 wall clock, with their corrections folded into BBB-GAP.md; status now: SURVIVED HOSTILE REVIEW, still kept off anything outward pending Caleb): the Thm 6.9 reduction step (showing Phi Gamma_1 reduced, then asserting Phi = Phi' Gamma_1) is repairable, so it is not where the proof fails; conjugating, Phi = Gamma_1^-1 y_1^m Phi_0 y_1^-m Gamma_1 (m larger than the reduced length of Phi_0; Gamma_1 begins with y_2^-1 in the free basis y_1, y_2, y_3 of K_4, and Phi_0 = [delta^-2, b] with delta = Delta_3^-2 Delta_4^2 lies in [K_4, K_4]) is a nontrivial Brunnian braid in K_4 in reduced proper-product form Phi' Gamma_1 with iota > 0 and rho_5(f(Phi)) = I at t = -1, meeting every hypothesis of Prop 6.7 and Cor 6.8; so no Gamma can have all the properties Prop 6.7 asserts, and the failure lies in Prop 6.7 (parity lemmas 6.2-6.6 or its Furthermore inequality) or in the paper's own unproved shortcut criterion certifying Gamma_1 as a proper product; Prop 5.1 is cleared, since parity forces minimal position by itself (m = |M(-1)| <= iota <= m). The referees also note that Prop 6.7 applies 'proper product' to f(Phi) Gamma whose first factor is not in K_5, and that the geometric Conditions 1 and 2 from the figures were not checked. The explicit Phi meets every hypothesis of Lemma 6.3, Prop 6.7 and Cor 6.8. Supporting lemmas in the note: under parity epsilon_i (-1)^(k_i) is constant along beta, so |M(-1)| = m = sum of |coefficients| (Lemma 1); M_(YX)(-1) = M_X(-1) whenever rho(Y) = I at t = -1, since the Moody polynomial is a sesquilinear pairing of homology classes and rho(Y) - I is divisible by t + 1 (Lemma 2). A repair would have to compare the full exponent multiset (under parity M(t) = +-p(-t) with p >= 0; the first m moments of p are blind on rho = I mod (t+1)^m). Consequence for the board: Burau_4 faithfulness is OPEN again; KL(4,2) and route N1, which rested on BBB, lose their support (both were parked already). No published critique exists; nothing has gone outward; contacting the authors is Caleb's call.

G2-ORTH / Theorem A (lane, new, referee-passed): for 3|3 curves c, c' on the bridge sphere, r(c,c') = |H(v_c, v_c')|^2 / Delta_3^2 is regular at zeta = exp(i pi/4) (Kasahara's t = -1) and equals (2D - 1)^2 there, D = det(x, y, x', y') for symplectic bases of the lifts' homology splittings (Kasahara Lemma 2.1: the zeta-specialization of W(6,0) is Lambda^2_0 H with the wedge pairing, v_c -> u_c = 2 x^y - omega, <u_c, u_c'> = 4D - 2). So no two spin-3/2 lines are orthogonal and the F6 mechanism (orthogonal pairs giving kernel elements) never fires; only curve-against-cap pairings vanish at zeta, exactly on homologically reducing curves (Prop D). Theorem B (lane): the kernel is nontrivial iff some Torelli f moves c_0 and fixes the line [v_(c_0)]; then [T_(c_0)^2, f] = T_gamma T_(f gamma)^-1 is in the kernel; it lies in every Kasahara F_k; one Laurent identity R_f(A) R_f(1/A) = Delta_2^2 decides a candidate.

G2-TWIST (lane's strongest open target; faithfulness-strength): put U_3 in standard 3-bridge position; if one full twist along a 3|3 curve c gives a link whose V equals V(U_3) up to a unit, then c is reducing (bounds a disk meeting the link once) and the twisted link is U_3; in the module, H(v_c, S) = 0 forces c reducing for S. It implies injectivity of c -> [v_c], hence genus-two faithfulness, hence W(5,1) and Burau_4 faithful; U (V detects U_3 among 3-bridge links) implies it; with the Haken-curve lemma it gives T for 3-bridge knots. Heegaard lane: Stab[c] = Hilden group iff U.

G2-SEP (Codex 151334; read and confirmed by Claude): Lemma C is TRUE for tangles in a finite-list form: given a 3|3 curve c and finitely many 3|3 curves c_1..c_m not isotopic to c, there is a trivial 3-tangle S for which c is reducing and no c_j is. Proof: lift to Sigma_2, cut along the separating lift gamma of c into once-punctured tori Q_1, Q_2; each gamma_j meets gamma (non-isotopic separating curves of Sigma_2 intersect, since a once-punctured torus has no essential separating curve), and in minimal position its segments are essential arcs of the Q_i, which close to nonzero primitive classes after capping; compress Sigma_2 x I along gamma: pi_1 = Z^2 * Z^2 and each gamma_j is a cyclically reduced alternating word with nonzero syllables; choose primitive ell_i(a,b) = a + N_i b avoiding the finitely many syllables (at most one forbidden N_i per syllable) and fill the inner tori killing ker ell_i; the result is a genus-two handlebody H with pi_1 = Z * Z in which gamma bounds the separating disk and every gamma_j survives as a nonempty reduced word, so bounds no disk. Realization: in each 3-punctured disk bounded by c take an arc between two punctures whose enclosing curve lifts to the chosen meridian slope (B_3 acts transitively on slopes of the capped 4-point sphere), join the remaining punctures by an arc crossing c once, push in: a trivial tangle with branched double cover H. Removes the separate Lemma C gap: G2-TWIST => injectivity of c -> [v_c] => genus-two faithfulness.

G2-DEEP (Codex 151334; read and confirmed by Claude): for a reducing pair (c_0, S) and every r >= 1 there is f_r in the r-th lower central subgroup of I_2 with F_r(A) = Hhat(S, rho(f_r) v_(c_0)) nonzero of order >= r at zeta; c_r = f_r(c_0) has the same homological splitting as c_0 and is nonreducing. Proof: G2-DENSE plus the commutator morphism and perfectness of SL_5 make every lower central subgroup Zariski dense; rho(gamma_r I_2) = I mod h^r; the regular function F(g) = Hhat(S, g v_(c_0)) vanishes at I and is not identically zero, so it is nonzero somewhere on each dense gamma_r, with order >= r there; nonzero pairing excludes reducing curves (easy half of the skein criterion). So no uniform vanishing-order cutoff exists even at fixed splitting; high-order vanishing is not evidence of an exact zero; a complexity-dependent bound remains possible and is what G2-TWIST needs.

G2-FINITE (Codex 150127; structure read by Claude, constants not independently checked against Endo and Turaev): for M = Mod(Sigma_2), I = I_2, D = [M, I], Z_pi = pi(I)/pi(D): |Z_pi| divides 10 for every finite quotient pi of M, and Z_(phi^(k)) = Z/10 for every finite-order Jones truncation k >= 1. Proof shape: E = M/D is a central extension of Sp(4,Z) by <T> (T a separating twist); Endo 2000 (Prop 3.1, Lemma 3.5: phi: M -> (1/5)Z, delta phi = tau, phi(T) = -4/5), Turaev 1984 ([tau] = 4c, c the restricted universal-cover class) give 4 eps = 5[tau] = 20c; pushing out by 4, the universal cover of Sp(4,Z) embeds with central generator z = T^5, and Deligne 1978 (every finite-index subgroup of that cover contains 2Z) puts T^10 in every finite-index subgroup of E. For truncations: rho_k(I) is integral unitriangular; an elementary lemma (every finite-index subgroup of a subgroup of UT_d(Z) contains a matrix congruence kernel) plus the first theorem forces rho_k(I)/rho_k(D) finite of order dividing 10, and Kasahara's first truncation gives 10 | order. Consequence: the strategy "unbounded Z_rho from deeper truncations" is closed; d_hat(F_(k+1)) = 10Z for all k; the generic Z_rho (Kasahara Problem 6.2) stays OPEN because d_hat of the intersection need not equal the intersection of the d_hat's. A kernel element x in ker rho cap I with d_hat(x) nonzero would give a Jones-trivial knot with nonzero signature (Casson-Mullins); none is known.

Lane items DEAD: F6 as a kernel source (no orthogonal pair); BBB-type parity counts for W(5,1) (blind on BI_5); extreme degree of H(v_c, v_c') controlled by i(c,c') (degree linear, intersection exponential); one-twist kernel candidates and single commutators of separating twists with distinct splittings; one-sided BBB and "Kasahara depth <= C|f|" (restate faithfulness); Cauchy-Schwarz equality as a route to the unknot. Status: W(5,1) OPEN; G2-TWIST the target, its missing piece a Jones-to-disk mechanism using exact global skein data with disk topology; Codex (resumed directly by Caleb at ~15:00) is active on it; the zrho sub-lane of the breakthrough chat is in referee stage.


### R. Z_rho sub-lane (15:28 wall clock; Caleb's structure chat, ~/jones-lanes/g2-proof/zrho/ZRHO.md; relayed to Codex as letter 152758): Kasahara's Problem 6.2 as a signature statement

Setup: I_2 is free on separating twists (Mess); d^ : I_2 -> Z the exponent sum in Mess's basis (d^(T_c) = 1; M_2-invariant; Ker d^ = [M_2, I_2]); N = Ker rho cap I_2 (holds every kernel element up to iota, Kasahara 2008 Thm 5.1); Z_rho = Z/d^(N). Problem 6.2 (Kasahara 2001; Morita 2006 for all genera) asks the order of Z_rho; open as far as the lane could read.
G2-ZRHO-CASSON (lane, TRUSTED, derived twice + exact script; constant not found stated in a source, Morita-Sakasai-Suzuki arXiv:1711.07855 the likely place): on I_2, lambda(M_phi) = -d^(phi)/10 + q(tau_2 phi), q linear, 1/10 = 1/(2(2g+1)) at g = 2; in Kasahara's notation d_1 = (12/5) d^ (his section-6 claim that q_f factors through tau_2 needs exactly this normalisation). Identity (TRUSTED): M_phi is the double branched cover of a 3-bridge knot K_phi (Birman-Hilden); Mullins gives 4 d^(phi) + 5 sigma(K_phi) = G(rho(phi)) with G built from V'(-1)/V(-1) and tau_2, both functions of rho(phi) (V a matrix coefficient of rho; Cor 1.2); checked on T(3,6n+1), n = -3..3. Repaired form: on N, sigma(K_kappa) = -(4/5) d^(kappa) (the brief's "d = 3 sigma + F" held only for the non-integral d = -(12/5) d^; the Meyer function restricts to -(4/5) d^ on I_2 although the Meyer cocycle vanishes on I_2 x I_2).
G2-ZRHO (TRUSTED): Z_rho is finite iff some kappa in N gives a Jones-trivial 3-bridge knot K_kappa with nonzero signature, the order then being (5/4) min |sigma|; order 10 would mean a knot with V = 1, sigma = -8, lambda(Sigma_2) = -1. T implies Z_rho = Z directly (kernel knots have V = 1, so under T they are unknots with sigma = 0), needing neither Ito nor the normal-intersection lemma. The Casson form of the equivalence is Kasahara's own section-6 sentence (rho carries lambda iff it carries d_1); the signature form and the direct implication from T were not found in the literature and may be folklore. Claude's read: with V = 1 the derivative term of Mullins vanishes and lambda(Sigma_2 K) = -sigma/8 up to convention, so sigma = (4/5) d^ up to sign; det 1 forces sigma = 0 mod 8, so d^(N) is contained in 10Z, consistent with G2-FINITE's d^(F_(k+1)) = 10Z.
Theorem A (lane; TRUSTED modulo Deligne's normalisation; = Codex's G2-FINITE by a second route): every finite quotient of M_2 sees at most Z/10 (M_2/[M_2, I_2] is a central Z-extension of Sp(4,Z) with class 5 lambda_1 up to sign and torsion; Deligne, confirmed for g >= 2 in Funar-Pitsch arXiv:1103.1855, kills twice the generator in every finite quotient of the universal extension), every Kasahara truncation gives exactly Z/10, and (Lemma B) so does every specialisation whose image of M_2 contains the image of I_2 with finite index, including every root of unity other than q = -1; if lambda_1 were twice Deligne's class the bound would be 20, boundedness survives either way. Where the 10 comes from: 5 = image of I_2 in H_1(M_2) = Z/10 (q = 1, separating twists scalar); 2 = parity, d^ mod 2 = sum of the ten even Birman-Craggs homomorphisms. Theorem A is a fact about M_2 alone (the identity map has infinite Z yet every finite quotient sees Z/10), so it is no evidence either way; it also shows Kasahara's truncation data cannot support his remark that order exactly 10 remains possible. No residual method (finite quotients, truncations, roots of unity) can decide Problem 6.2: the global-only principle G1/G2 again.
Scalar normal form (elementary): rho(T_c) = t^6 R_c with R_c a pseudo-reflection; Z_rho = Z/m iff the scalars in <R_c> are generated by t^(-6m) I; no two distinct R_c commute or braid, so a scalar needs a long global word (at least 10 letters whose root lines span).
Fit with the W(5,1) lane: commutator kernel elements [T_c^2, f] have d^ = 0 and sigma = 0, so a signature can only certify a counterexample from a non-commutator kernel element; the reformulation says nothing about commutators and cannot decide T either way.
Sharpest open statement (infinite side of 6.2): every kappa in N has d^(kappa) = 0; equivalently every Jones-trivial kernel knot has signature 0; equivalently <R_c> contains no scalar other than I; equivalently the Casson invariant of M_phi is a function of rho(phi). Follows from T and from faithfulness of the [2,2,1] representation of B_5; does not imply T. The reason "BBB closes Kasahara's route to a kernel" is WITHDRAWN (the gap). Any proof must be non-residual; the remaining theory route the lane names is global: whether lambda_1 pulls back to zero in H^2(rho(M_2); Q). Question sent to Codex: can the W(5,1) analysis reach d^(N) = 0 without full faithfulness, i.e. a structural reason why no product of the R_c is a nontrivial scalar at generic q?
DEAD (lane): d^ mod 25 at t = zeta_25 and a 5-adic tower (decided in advance by Lemma B); the verdict "every reason points to Z_rho = Z" (rested on T and pairwise relations); "finite Z_rho would be far stronger than not-T" (one Jones-trivial knot with sigma nonzero gives unbounded signature by connected sum); the identity lane's section-9 lead (vanishes identically on N); the Hodge-winding crux (restates d^|_N = 0). Unchecked sketches: Toledo-blindness; sigma(K_.) a homomorphism on I_2.


### S. Codex 153729 (15:42 wall clock): the pair alternative for spin lines (G2-PAIR, G2-PAIR-COMM); read and confirmed by Claude

G2-PAIR (TRUSTED). Let P, Q be the spin-line projectors of two 3|3 curves c, d (orthogonal projectors for the invariant form H onto [v_c], [v_d]) and r = tr(PQ) = R(A) R(A^-1) / Delta_2^2 (Delta_2 = A^4 + 1 + A^-4, R integral Laurent; the lane's rainbow formula). G2-ORTH gives r nonzero; distinct lines give r different from 1 (Cauchy-Schwarz equality on the definite unit-circle arc would force identical lines), so U = span(v_c, v_d) is a nondegenerate 2-plane and W = U + U-perp with U-perp = ker P cap ker Q. On U the downstairs twists are X = A^6 P + A^-6 (1 - P), Y = A^6 Q + A^-6 (1 - Q) (both act as the scalar A^-6 on U-perp). Degree bound: the numerators of r and 1 - r are nonzero reciprocal Laurent polynomials (top degree >= 0) over the common denominator of degree 8, so d = deg r and e = deg(1 - r) satisfy d, e >= -8 and |d - e| <= 8 (from r + (1 - r) = 1: positive degrees coincide; otherwise both lie in [-8, 0]). For a cyclically reduced word X^(a_1) Y^(b_1) ... X^(a_k) Y^(b_k) with nonzero exponents, expanding every power by its two projectors gives trace coefficients that are exactly +- r^u (1 - r)^v with u + v = k (diagonalize P; Q = [[r, b],[c, 1 - r]] with bc = r(1 - r); the cyclic trace pairs off-diagonal b's with c's), and one projector flip changes u by at most one. Choosing the larger eigenvalue in every block gives a term of eigenvalue degree >= 12k; s flips lose >= 12s and gain <= 8s, so that term is uniquely of highest degree and the trace has degree >= 4k: nonconstant. With det = 1 on U, a scalar word would have constant trace +-2, so no nontrivial word is scalar on U; on W, scalarity on U-perp forces exponent sum zero and the same contradiction. Hence <X, Y> is projectively free; for the upstairs separating twists (squares) the same proof runs with eigenvalues A^(+-12), flip gap 24 - 8, trace degree >= 16k. Concrete subclass: two separating curves with different unordered integral homological splittings have distinct spin lines at the symplectic endpoint, so the Jones representation is projectively faithful on their two-generator subgroup with no unknown premise; same-splitting pairs are covered whenever their quantum lines are distinct. Scope: a pair alternative (coincide or freely generate), NOT curve injectivity; distinct curves with identical spin lines remain possible and would be a basic kernel witness. Prior art: Church-Pixton arXiv:0804.3633 Thm 6.1 (commute-or-free for Magnus images of positive separating multitwists; square-zero operators and a transcendental trace, not applicable to these semisimple operators); the new ingredient is the degree comparison 12 > 8; novelty not determined.
G2-PAIR-COMM (TRUSTED). Let f be a nonidentity commutator-subgroup word in T_c = tau_c^2 and T_d = tau_d^2 with distinct seed lines. On U, det f = 1 and tr f differs from 2 (nonconstant trace), so f has no eigenvalue 1 on U; on U-perp f = I. At zeta both raw squared twists are -I (the annihilating identity), so rho(f) = I + O((A - zeta)^2) for a commutator word. If f fixes a rational line with eigenvalue lambda, then lambda and 1/lambda are integral over the integrally closed Z[A^+-1] (f and f^-1 are integral Laurent matrices), so lambda = +-A^j; a rational eigenvector regular and nonzero at zeta gives lambda(zeta) = 1 and lambda'(zeta) = 0, hence lambda = 1; eigenvalue 1 is absent on U and f preserves U and U-perp, so every invariant line lies in U-perp; G2-ORTH keeps every spin line out of U-perp (it would be orthogonal to v_c). So f fixes no spin line, and [T_e, f] is not a kernel element for any third curve e (projective commutation is no loophole: T_e has eigenvalue multiplicities 1 and 4, so conjugation cannot change it by a nontrivial scalar). This closes the report's Section 4 two-generator commutator class at every word length, with the single exception that the two seed lines already coincide (which would itself be a kernel witness). Claude's read: checked the coefficient lemma, the degree inequalities, the det/trace exclusion of eigenvalue 1, the integrality step (Z[A^+-1] a UFD, units +-A^j), and the second-order endpoint identity.
G2-ZRHO, two-generator corollary: for any subgroup generated by two separating twists, a projectively trivial Jones word has total separating-twist exponent zero (distinct lines: free; coincident lines: a power of the common operator with eigenvalue ratio A^(24 s), scalar iff s = 0), so d^ vanishes on the kernel of every such subgroup. A genuine special case of the scalar-product question; nothing for three or more lines, nothing for generic Z_rho; the two-dimensional coefficient formula is what has not been extended. Codex: bank it, stop the two-generator candidate search, no three-generator generalization without a replacement for the projector coefficient lemma. G2-TWIST, curve-line injectivity, W(5,1), the Haken-curve statement and T remain OPEN.

G2-ZRHO-SPAN (Codex 154356, 15:46 wall clock; read and confirmed by Claude): elementary proper-span obstruction. Rescale each separating-twist operator to R_i = I + (A^24 - 1) P_i with P_i the rank-one spin-line projector; let U be the span of the seed lines. If dim U < 5 then U-perp (for H) is nonzero even when U is degenerate, every R_i^(+-1) fixes U-perp pointwise, so a word whose R-product is a scalar has scalar 1; its determinant is A^(24 s) with s the total signed exponent, hence s = 0; d^ = s on words in these twists, so d^ vanishes on the Jones kernel (even the projective kernel) of any subgroup generated by separating twists with proper-span spin lines, in particular on ANY four separating twists. Needs no G2-ORTH, freeness, Casson or signature input. Sharp remaining obstruction for the scalar-product question: a kernel element with nonzero d^ must use spin lines spanning all five dimensions in every separating-twist expression, hence at least five distinct spin lines; with full span determinants give only lambda^5 = A^(24 s), no conclusion. Supersedes the two-generator exponent-sum corollary of G2-PAIR (whose freeness and fixed-line conclusions stand, not extended beyond two generators); zero-exponent kernels in proper-span subgroups are not excluded. Generic d^(N) = 0, Z_rho, W(5,1), G2-TWIST, T: OPEN.


### T. Adversarial pass (15:49 wall clock; Caleb's third chat, ~/jones-adversarial-pass/: 5 verifiers, process audit, 3 designers, 6 referees): verdict, change of order, five items

Verdict: the morning logged 34 correct outputs (7 equivalences, 18 barriers, 8 dictionaries, 1 sieve) and no narrowing of T; the afternoon did better because each lane held one precise question with a referee before banking (G2-SEP, G2-FINITE, Theorem A, G2-ZRHO, the BBB gap); the coordinated target G2-TWIST had no holder and no mechanism while both sides had ruled out every computation that could test one.
CHANGE OF ORDER (adopted on the board; Caleb governs if he says otherwise): Burau_4 faithfulness is the smallest open rung (G2-TWIST, W(5,1), genus-two faithfulness and T all imply it; if it fails, T fails by Ito Cor 3) and has the most concrete architecture: if BBB's Lemmas 6.3-6.6 hold, two-sided parity removes all cancellation (Lemma 4.2) and the step that cannot work on BI_4 is the comparison of counts (t = -1 values); a kernel element has infinite (t+1)-adic depth, so a repair must control moments of every order (the full Moody comparison of Thm 2.3). G2-TWIST second in line.
BBB-MULTISET (statement held by Codex; source ~/jones-lanes/g2-proof/ref-transfer/ref-transfer.md section 4): for every nontrivial proper product Phi in Brun_4 cap BI_4, with Gamma the push from Prop 6.7 before its Furthermore modification (Gamma = gamma_2 gamma_1: gamma_1 from p_5 to a point q in the innermost 4-disk missing beta, gamma_2 back crossing each 4-disk's beta-side once), the exponent multisets of M_(f(Phi) Gamma) and M_Gamma differ up to translation. Under parity each multiset is the coefficient vector of M(-t); both polynomials are Burau matrix entries once Gamma is a braid word. Includes the claim that no such Phi has (t+1)-adic depth above its intersection number. First lemma candidate: a span inequality between the two arcs (the span is the range of the height function k_i, steps +-|P(Delta_i)| by Lemma 3.1; 4-disk steps become 5-disk steps on f(Phi) Gamma); hard, since it concerns extreme degrees of Burau_4 images. First check (Claude, attacker): construct Gamma for the explicit Phi of BBB-GAP.md and compare the two polynomials: both signs in some M(-t) => parity fails there (error in Lemmas 6.3-6.6); equal multisets => the unmodified push is dead; different => the proof attempt starts.
Five items recorded (reports in ~/jones-adversarial-pass/workflow-results.json, scripts in work/): (1) the collision value N = +-A^k [3] passes every Hermitian, integrality and root-of-unity test available, including Theorem A at zeta_8 and the (A^4 - 1)^2 divisibility, and so does the non-collision value 2A^4 + A^-4: no argument built only from those tests can prove the span bound or exclude a collision. (2) Schottky bounds in Mess word length are FALSE: c = boundary of {1,2,3}, x = s_3(c), c' = s_3^2(c), z = s_2 s_3^-1(c) (four splittings; squared twists generate F_4), B = R_c R_x R_c' R_x^-1 R_c^-1 R_z gives D(B^4) = 200 < 208; a conjugated letter keeps pair overlap at most 8 for any conjugator length. (3) N is not sign-coherent for 38 percent of partition-preserving pairs; along Torelli words the span adds while intersection numbers multiply, so naive adequacy arguments fail. (4) On 1 < delta < sqrt 2 the form has signature (4,1), but by G2-DENSE the image is dense there, so complex hyperbolic geometry gives no discreteness (Funar arXiv:2009.05961 section 2.6 puts the genus-two representation in PU(1,4)). (5) The 3-bridge plat argument (a genus-two kernel gives 3-bridge knots with V = 1) is complete with two fixes: Ichihara-Saito arXiv:1209.0097 Claims 1-2 for the distance growth, and conjugation by tau_c^N so both laminations avoid the disk sets; a second proof of Kasahara 2008 plus Ito. Dead designs from the pass: CH^4 complex reflections / Haken form H_n; the half-twist "foreign line" criterion (= the existing collision certificate); the Torelli Schottky inequality (false on the explicit word; the filling-triple fact survives numerically).
What a breakthrough has to contain (the pass's criteria, recorded as a checklist): see below t = -1 (V = 1 knots have determinant 1, kernel elements are Torelli, and t = -1 sees only the homology of a double branched cover); control exact extreme coefficients; use that K is classical (links with the unlink's polynomial and virtual knots with V = 1 exist); the Khovanov/Floer versions of several board statements are known (Kronheimer-Mrowka, Batson-Seed, Khovanov-Seidel), and the open part is always that a difference the categorified invariant sees can vanish in the Euler characteristic; for G2-TWIST the open part is exactly the Eliahou-Kauffman-Thistlethwaite phenomenon restricted to 3-bridge links.


### U. Codex 155456 (15:59 wall clock): G2-ORBIT, a Jones-to-disk theorem on two-twist orbits; read and confirmed by Claude

G2-ORBIT (TRUSTED). Let S be a trivial three-tangle, a a curve reducing for S (so H(S, v_a) = 0), and d a 3|3 curve with H(S, v_d) nonzero (sufficient homological condition: L_S cap P_d^hom = 0, which makes the divided endpoint pairing a nonzero determinant). Let G = <T_a, T_d> (upstairs separating twists). Then for EVERY f in G: H(S, v_(f(a))) = 0 iff f lies in <T_a> iff f(a) = a iff f(a) is reducing for S. Proof: U = span(v_a, v_d) is a nondegenerate two-plane preserved by G; the cap functional l = H(S, .) vanishes on v_a but not on v_d, so ker l cap U = [v_a]; a zero at v_(f(a)) = rho(f) v_a (up to scalar) forces rho(f) to preserve the line [v_a], hence (as an H-isometry) to commute with P_a and with rho(T_a); by G2-PAIR (rho projectively faithful on G, which is free on T_a, T_d since the two lines are distinct) this gives [f, T_a] = 1 in the free group, so f lies in the centralizer <T_a>; the converse is the easy direction (reducing implies zero pairing). If d is itself reducing both twists extend over the tangle and the whole orbit is reducing; a nonreducing d with zero pairing remains the unresolved crux and is not assumed away.
Cap stabilizer and unlink detection (TRUSTED): every rational projective line outside U-perp has cyclic stabilizer in G (two noncommuting stabilizing elements would give a nonidentity commutator fixing the line, against G2-PAIR-COMM; abelian subgroups of F_2 are cyclic). The cap line [S] is outside U-perp (H(S, v_d) nonzero) and its stabilizer contains the primitive element T_a, so Stab_G([S]) = G cap HandlebodyGroup(S) = <T_a> (actual extension preserves the cap line; powers of T_a extend by the reducing disk). For the three-bridge plat L_f with S above and below a pure braid lift of f: V(L_f) = V(U_3) up to a monomial unit iff rho(f) S is proportional to S (Cauchy-Schwarz equality on the definite arc; Jones Prop 15.6) iff f in <T_a> iff L_f = U_3. So the Jones polynomial detects the three-component unlink in every such two-twist plat family, without uniqueness of the bridge sphere; for a general pair with distinct spin lines the Jones-unlink fibre inside G is cyclic unless S lies in U-perp, where all of G fixes it.
Explicit exact orders (sharpening G2-DEEP, constructive): a = boundary of {1,2,3}, S = D4 = (16)(23)(45), d = sigma_3(a); rainbow duality (e_3 D4 = D5) gives H(S, v_a) = 0 and H(S, v_d) = A^-1 Delta_3. With f_1 = T_d and f_(r+1) = [T_a, f_r] (T_a = (sigma_1 sigma_2)^6, T_d = sigma_3 T_a sigma_3^-1), F_r = Hhat(S, rho(f_r) v_a) is nonzero of order EXACTLY r at zeta, f_r lies in gamma_r(I_2), and every f_r(a) has a's integral homological splitting and is nonreducing. Leading term: with h = (A - zeta)/zeta and -rho(T_i) = I + h B_i + ..., B_i = 24 P_i(zeta) - 12 I, one has rho(f_r) = I + h^r ad_(B_a)^(r-1)(B_d) + O(h^(r+1)) for r >= 2; l_0 P_a = 0 and P_a v_a = v_a give l_0 [B_a, X] v_a = -24 l_0 X v_a (checked: (0 - 12 l_0 X v_a) - (24 - 12) l_0 X v_a), so the leading coefficient is 24 (-24)^(r-1) C with C = l_0 P_d v_a nonzero (seed identity plus G2-ORTH); for r = 1 it is -24 C. Arbitrarily deep approximate zeros that are rigorously never exact zeros; no density theorem or search needed.
The gap, at the failing step: on a span of three independent spin lines the cap functional's kernel is already two-dimensional, so zero pairing no longer pins a line and the centralizer proof stops; and there is no topological normal form putting every homologically reducing curve into a certified two-twist orbit with a known reducing anchor (after a nonreducing move the image cannot serve as the next anchor). First place in the day where skein noncancellation became a disk statement on a controlled topological class by an exact global argument. General G2-TWIST, W(5,1), generic Z_rho, T: OPEN.


### V. Codex161119: BBB packet comparison and bundled-puncture repair

BBB-MULTISET stays OPEN, held by Codex; Claude retains the first explicit Gamma construction. The geometric candidate is matched-ribbon extraction: equal anchored height multisets should yield an embedded puncture-free permitted bigon-forming polygon after undoing the nested push ribbons. Both embedded extraction and the permitted polygon type are unproved. The signed four-disk height increment is sgn(W_i), not unsigned nesting depth.

BBB-BUNDLE (TRUSTED, written proof and internal read; Claude read pending): weight old punctures by t and the auxiliary puncture by u. The old braid acts on the relative cover module as rho_4(Phi)(t) direct-sum 1, so a generic kernel forces the colored difference D(t,u)=0. Bundling q auxiliary punctures and pushing their small disk along the same Gamma realizes D(t,t^q), with no new intersections of the old arcs with alpha. If D is nonzero, every odd q greater than its old-t exponent span is nonzero and preserves parity. Thus a q=1 collapse can have a specified ordinary stabilized repair. On the BI_4 slice D(-1,u)=0: counts agree in every u-packet, and old depth r forces every packet difference divisible by (t+1)^r. Full old-height profiles, not auxiliary counts, are the proposed separator. No universal nonvanishing is proved. Evidence: work/global_design/bbb_multiset_packets.md; reply161119.

Scope of section T's five relayed findings (Codex read of the reports, without rerunning their scripts): the two listed Laurent values are formal test survivors, not both asserted realized by curves; 38 percent is the reported sample frequency; density is at the generic/transcendental parameters covered by G2-DENSE, not all roots of unity; the Mess-length counterexample does not refute G2-PAIR's two-generator trace-degree estimate; the repaired three-bridge argument remains the external lane's checked result, with its distance citations not freshly audited here.

Codex 161119 (16:13 wall clock), holder's first pass on BBB-MULTISET; read by Claude. (1) Two-variable refinement: weight p_1..p_4 by t and the auxiliary p_5 by u (same Fox matrices, weights (t,t,t,t,u), common based lifts), D(t,u) = M_(f(Phi) Gamma)(t,u) - M_Gamma(t,u); on the Z^2 cover f(Phi) acts as rho_4(Phi)(t) + 1, so rho_4(Phi) = I forces D = 0 with no colored-faithfulness premise; Gamma is pure so it preserves the coloring; the repaired arc's exponent is the pair (k_i, s_i) with s_i the SIGNED sum of preceding four-disk crossings (a negative four-disk changes the increment -4 to (-4,-1), ordinary specialization -5; the paper's section 7 display confirms this and its prose "increase by 1" has the opposite sign). Constraint: rho_4(Phi)(-1) = I gives D(-1,u) = 0, so every fixed-u packet has equal counts on the two sides, and depth r makes every packet difference divisible by (t+1)^r: neither the auxiliary span nor the mass on its outermost face can separate; the possible separator is the full old-t height profile within a face. (2) BBB-BUNDLE (TRUSTED; Claude checked the collision argument): replace p_5 by q nearby punctures in a small disk pushed along the same Gamma; with the old arcs and lifts unchanged outside the moving disk, M^(q)(t) = M(t, t^q), no extra intersections with alpha, odd q preserves parity; if D is nonzero with old-t exponent range B then for every q > B, D(t, t^q) is nonzero (a collision would need a - a' = q(b' - b) with |a - a'| <= B < q), so every odd q > B gives an ordinary stabilized Burau witness for the original Phi even if q = 1 cancels; if D = 0 identically all bundles are blind. (3) Geometric first lemma (OPEN, matched-ribbon extraction): if the anchored height multisets of the two compared arcs agree, choose a height-preserving matching with an outermost matched loop which, after undoing the push ribbons, is an embedded bigon-forming polygon forbidden by properness; equal heights give zero total puncture winding; the missing geometry is why an embedded loop can be extracted and why it becomes a permitted polygon type; nestedness is the proposed mechanism. Normalization: kernel exclusion needs only anchored inequality (common based lifts), weaker than the held "up to translation" form, retained OPEN. Claude's explicit Gamma construction (agent running) is told to retain the two winding labels and report D(t,u).

Codex 162630 (16:28 wall clock), BBB-LOCAL (TRUSTED; read by Claude at sketch level): a localization lemma using Brunnianness that weakens the geometric endpoint of matched-ribbon extraction. Let b_0 = b Gamma and b_1 = b f(Phi) Gamma with Phi Brunnian on p_1..p_4 and b a boundary-to-p_3 arc. Suppose b_0 and b_1 coincide outside pairwise disjoint disks E_i, each arc passing through each disk exactly once with the same boundary endpoints, each disk omitting at least one of p_1, p_2, p_4, the disks avoiding p_3 except possibly one terminal disk containing p_3 and the two terminal segments. Then b_0 and b_1 are isotopic and Phi = 1. Proof: the common exterior pieces and the disks form a chain, so pi_1 of a thin neighbourhood is the free product of the punctured-disk groups (plus a separate p_3-peripheral factor if there is no terminal disk), with access-path conjugations absorbed in a tree-based decomposition; the comparison loop b_1^-1 b_0 has ONE syllable per disk factor; for an ordinary disk delete an old puncture outside it: Brunnianness makes the whole comparison an isotopy while that disk's factor stays injective, and a free-product word with a single nontrivial syllable in an injective factor cannot be trivial, so the syllable was trivial; repeat for every ordinary disk; for a terminal disk the same argument makes its syllable p_3-peripheral, which is isotopy of the terminal arcs; disjoint supports give simultaneous isotopies; undoing Gamma, f(Phi) fixes b, forgetting p_3 is injective on the stabilizer of a boundary-to-p_3 arc (cut along the fixed arc), and Brunnianness gives Phi = 1; no pseudo-Anosov input. Repaired open implication for BBB-MULTISET: equal anchored coloured profiles => simultaneous localization into once-visited proper punctured regions (regions may contain punctures, comparison loops may self-intersect: weaker than an empty chronologically permitted BBB polygon). Converse remarks: a nontrivial Brunnian comparison in a once-per-disk decomposition must have a disk containing all of p_1, p_2, p_4; repeated visits allow separate factors to return and form commutators; so the remaining geometry is whether equal profiles force a decomposition without full-support disks or returns (unproved). Local anchor: in the puncture-free annular band between nested four-disks the core has colour (4,0) (or (4,1) after repair) and colouring is injective on its fundamental group, so equal-displacement arcs with fixed endpoints are isotopic within the band. Calibration (why ordering alone fails): a twist about a curve enclosing p_3, p_4 disjoint from alpha and p_5 changes a boundary-to-p_3 arc crossing it but fixes alpha and its based lift, so old and new arcs have the same signed coloured crossings with alpha in the same order with the same labels without being isotopic; not Brunnian, no refutation of the held statement. BBB p. 16 (after Lemma 6.2) warns that individual disk isotopies cannot in general be performed simultaneously, so their finite disk-type list does not supply the localization. Status: BBB-MULTISET OPEN (holder Codex) with proper-region localization as a sufficient geometric endpoint; polynomial equality => localization is the OPEN crux; faithfulness unchanged; Claude's explicit Gamma construction still running.


### W. Codex163948: an exact nested-return family inside Brunnian BI4

BBB-RETURN (TRUSTED, written proof/internal read, main Claude read pending). Let r,s be nonzero, u in B3, b0=[u,sigma3^(2r)] and bj=[Delta3^(4s),b_(j-1)]. The generic Burau image of b1 is identity exactly when u is a sigma1 power; otherwise every tower image is nonidentity and pairwise distinct. For nontrivial Brunnian3 u, all bj are Brunnian4 and bj is identity modulo (t+1)^j. The proof splits the old three-strand reduced plane from its fixed complement; the rank-one crossing overlap alpha=t/(1+t+t²) gives nonzero factor (1+t²+t^(2r+1))/(1+t+t²). The exact Burau3 column stabilizer then forces the exceptional sigma1 power. At t=1, repeated commutators act by ad of the plane projector on a nonzero off-diagonal leading term, giving exact orders k+j. These are GLOBAL nonkernel statements, not deductions from finite near-zero checks. Scope excludes arbitrary products, a universal normal form, and the differently placed Phi0 of BBB-GAP. Details: work/global_design/bbb_mixed_returns.md; reply163948.

Direct confinement attempt remains OPEN: Lemma6.6 permits four-disk/three-disk and four-disk/one-disk splices; order along gamma2 does not imply order along the compared arcs. No global counterexample to the Brunnian proper-push claim was constructed, and the held target is not withdrawn.

Codex 163948 (16:42 wall clock), BBB-RETURN (TRUSTED; read by Claude): an explicit detected family inside Brun_4 cap BI_4 of unbounded (t+1)-adic depth. For u in B_3 and nonzero integers r, s let b_0 = [u, sigma_3^(2r)] and b_j = [Delta_3^(4s), b_(j-1)] (Delta_3^2 = (sigma_1 sigma_2)^3). Then (1) rho_4(b_1) = I iff u is a power of sigma_1; (2) otherwise every rho_4(b_j) is nonidentity and the images are pairwise distinct; (3) if u is nontrivial Brunnian on three strands (seed u = [A_13, A_23]) every b_j is Brunnian on four strands with rho_4(b_j) = I mod (t+1)^j for j >= 1. Mechanism (unreduced convention sigma_i -> [[1-t, t],[1, 0]]): with the invariant Hermitian form H = [[1,-1,-t],[-1,1,-1],[-t^-1,-1,1]] on three strands, U = rho_3(u) fixing e_3 also fixes z = (1,1,1), hence the plane span(z, e_3), hence (unitarity) its orthogonal line, where the determinant fixes the eigenvalue, so U = rho_3(sigma_1^(exp u)) and Burau_3 faithfulness gives u in <sigma_1>. In four dimensions take E = ker(1, t, t^2) on the old coordinates and F = span(z_3, e_4): S = rho(Delta_3^(4s)) = t^(6s) I_E + I_F, U = U_E + I_F, R = rho(sigma_3^(2r)) = I + c P with c = t^(2r) - 1, P = v l, v = (0,0,-t,1), l = (0,0,-1,1)/(t+1); the E/F overlap of v and l is alpha = t/(1+t+t^2); if B = [U, R] commutes with S its E<-F block vanishes, which first forces U_E x = lambda x and then B_EF = (c/(1+c))(1 + c alpha)(lambda - 1) x b with 1 + c alpha = (1 + t^2 + t^(2r+1))/(1 + t + t^2), a sum of positive monomials, nonzero for every r; so lambda = 1, U e_3 = e_3, u in <sigma_1>. Tower: at t = 1 with h = t - 1 and h^k C the first nonzero off-diagonal term of B_0 - I, B_j - I = h^(k+j) (6s)^j ad_J^j(C) + O(h^(k+j+1)) with J the E-projector, and ad_J^2 = I on off-diagonal matrices, so the orders k + j are exact; at t = -1, S = I and the commutator filtration gives order >= j; Brunnian by strand deletion for b_0 and normality for the tower. Claude's read: the fixed-plane argument, the block structure of S, U, R, the coefficient 1 + c alpha, the ad_J iteration and the depth count check; the explicit E/F block formula is Astra's calculation with an internal read. Scope: the outer full twist must sit on the same three-strand block as u; not identified with Phi_0 (differently placed), not a certificate for any Gamma, not a replacement for Claude's test; products of such words and general Brunnian BI_4 braids uncovered. Geometric attempt that failed (recorded as the identified gap in extremal-packet confinement): BBB's Lemma 6.6 permits the four-disk side to splice through an original beta* segment to a {p_1,p_2,p_3}-disk or a {p_4}-disk, changing old height without changing auxiliary height, and nesting orders crossings along gamma_2 without identifying that order with the order along the compared arcs. BBB-MULTISET OPEN, holder Codex.

Codex 165410 (16:56 wall clock), BBB-PAIR (TRUSTED; read by Claude) and the three-axis cancellation. Let R = rho_4(sigma_3^2) = I + (t^2 - 1) P and R_u = rho_4(u) R rho_4(u)^-1 for u in the old B_3 (return axes). Any two distinct axes freely generate in the generic Burau_4 image, projectively as well; equal axes occur exactly when u^-1 v is a power of sigma_1 (BBB-RETURN's fixed-vector lemma). Proof: G2-PAIR's two-dimensional coefficient lemma with r = tr(P_u P_v), common reciprocal denominator t + 2 + t^-1, so deg r, deg(1 - r) >= -1 and they differ by at most 1; neither r nor 1 - r vanishes (permutation specialization; positivity on the reduced summand near t = 1); normalizing both operators on U to eigenvalues t, t^-1, a spectral flip loses at least two eigenvalue degrees and gains at most one coefficient degree, so the maximal channel survives with trace degree >= k for a cyclic alternating word of 2k blocks. So arbitrary reduced products on two return axes are detected.
